README.md

Superstorm · Blackwell model atlas

Eight model-family reports. Six company directories. An 8×B300 research baseline, audited October 15, 2026.

Begin with START_HERE, read the comparison, then open the model relevant to your workload. GLOSSARY explains the acronyms and gives everyday analogies. LIMITATIONS states exactly what was and was not verified. The accompanying ATLAS.html is an offline reading edition.

Company → architecture → precision → runtime

CompanyModelStarting hypothesis
moonshot-aiKimi K3SGLang initial candidate for NVIDIA mixed NVFP4; compare vLLM and SGLang for native MXFP4
z-aiGLM 5.3vLLM FP8 and RedHat/Inferact NVFP4 first; SGLang challenger
z-aiGLM 5.3 FlashSGLang Blackwell FP8 initial candidate; vLLM FP8 and NVFP4 head-to-head
deepseekDeepSeek V4 Flash 0731vLLM TP1/DP4+EP baseline and SGLang documented TP4 comparator; qualify eight-GPU adaptations
alibaba-qwenQwen 3.8 27BCompare vLLM and SGLang TP1 BF16, FP8 and NVIDIA mixed NVFP4; no measured winner
alibaba-qwenQwen 3.8 Flash NextvLLM TP8 baseline for the NVIDIA mixed export; SGLang documented TP1 candidate, unverified on B300
googleGemma 4 31B ITvLLM TP1 BF16/FP8/NVFP4 comparison; SGLang challenger with export-level probe
mistral-aiMistral Medium 3.5vLLM publisher TP8 baseline; compare TP2/4 and publisher-documented SGLang at equal workload

Each model directory contains an architecture report, precision report, serving report, validation plan, source index and machine-readable record. Quantized NVIDIA/RedHat/Inferact derivatives live under their original model family, so the same architecture is not counted repeatedly.

Precision and evidence boundaries

Kimi K3's native release is MXFP4/MXFP8, not NVFP4; NVIDIA's NVFP4 export is a different mixed checkpoint. An ideal all-FP8 2.8T target exceeds the advertised 2.304 TB memory of eight 288 GB GPUs before caches. DeepSeek's July-31 release uses its own mixed FP4/FP8 and DSpark path. Qwen Flash Next's 125B core omits a separately reported 51B n-gram table and 4B MTP module. “Fits in memory” and “fastest” remain different claims. kimi-k3-CARD HW deepseek-v4-flash-0731-CFG qwen3.8-flash-next-CARD

Reading and implementation map

Hardware covers B300 identity, memory and topology. Memory math separates weights, token KV and recurrent state. Precision and kernels explains format versus execution. Framework decisions, vLLM source review, SGLang source review and KServe architecture justify the runtime/control-plane split. Nightly readiness tracks current source versus released binaries.

Profiles contain 34 launch experiments, including blocked/probe statuses rather than fake guarantees. Benchmark protocol defines fair comparisons. Evidence includes source IDs, upstream references and retrieval limitations.

The experiment roadmap connects stable/main/nightly changes to an ordered B300 investigation. Detailed vLLM experiments, SGLang experiments and KServe experiments explain mechanisms, controls, metrics and rejection criteria. Copy the run-record template to plan a reproducible comparison; these are proposed experiments, not measured results.

The engine update record documents the fresh research, source/version distinctions and independent review of this expansion.

python scripts/validate_repo.py
python -m unittest discover -s tests -v
python scripts/memory.py weights --parameters-b 2800 --format fp8
python scripts/preflight.py --output preflight.local.json
python scripts/launch.py --profile profiles/qwen3.8-27b.bf16.vllm.baseline.json
python scripts/clone_upstreams.py
python scripts/fetch_metadata.py --help

The last three commands are safe discovery/dry-run paths; neither weights nor external resources are created by default. Engine execution requires explicit gates and a locally pinned model. KServe rendering emits JSON only and requires an immutable image digest and existing model PVC. Installation, downloads and deployment are deliberate operator actions, not automatic side effects.

Snapshot and validation

vLLM latest release retrieved: 0.31.0, inspected source f8460ef23659afd69046a81529ec2295e4c3f686. SGLang latest release retrieved: 0.5.21, inspected source 877be2974aa151a984e52822c028276c2b1e9303. KServe latest release retrieved: 0.21.0. These are release observations and retained source-inspection commits. Separately observed current main references and nightly index metadata are recorded in the evidence lock; no container image was tested. VREL VHEAD GREL GHEAD KREL

See VALIDATION for actual local test results and the audit report for findings and coverage. No GPU serving results were measured. The original assembly's clone failures are retained as historical evidence; direct HTTPS retrieval and Git fetch succeeded during this audit. Selected immutable metadata and source files were rechecked. Complete upstream trees and model weight tensors are not bundled.

The primary reusable data are catalog/models.json, catalog/profiles.json, evidence/sources.json and evidence/upstreams.lock.json. Original authored material uses LICENSE; upstream code, model weights and derivative checkpoints retain their own terms.

Regenerate the offline edition with python3 scripts/build_atlas.py; use python3 scripts/build_atlas.py --check to detect stale content. Embedded source hashes identify the Markdown used to build each article.

START_HERE.md

Start here: a model-serving factory

You do not need to know GPU programming to navigate this repository. The core distinction is the model is the trained machine; the engine operates it; the platform keeps the service available.

One request, from question to answer

A tokenizer turns text into numbered pieces called tokens. An embedding turns each number into a vector, a list of numerical features. The model repeatedly transforms those features through its layers. Attention retrieves useful information from earlier tokens. A feed-forward block performs another learned transformation. A final head produces scores for possible next tokens, and the sampler chooses a token. This repeats until a stopping condition is met. Images require an additional encoder and preprocessing path. The exact implementation varies across the eight model reports. qwen3.8-27b-CARD gemma-4-31b-it-CFG mistral-medium-3.5-CFG

Prefill is reading the question and existing conversation. Decode is writing the answer a piece at a time. A restaurant analogy helps: preparing all ingredients is not the same job as plating each course. An optimization that speeds preparation might slow service if it monopolizes the same kitchen.

Five objects that must not be confused

ObjectIn this repositoryAnalogy
ArchitectureLayers, attention, experts, state, vision and output pathDesign of the machine
CheckpointA particular set of trained and possibly quantized weightsThe calibrated machine, not just its blueprint
vLLM / SGLangScheduling, cache management and GPU executionFactory operating system
CUDA / kernelsThe operations executed on the GPUMotors, gears and specialized tools
KServeKubernetes resources, rollout and routing lifecycleWarehouse and dispatch management

KServe is not a third arithmetic engine competing with vLLM and SGLang. It can manage a service whose container runs one of them. Advanced LLM orchestration has its own resources and integration requirements. KS KSRT KSLLM

Dense versus mixture of experts

A dense model uses its ordinary feed-forward blocks for every token. An MoE model routes tokens through a selected subset of experts. A building with 896 specialist offices may consult only 16 offices for a token, but the others still occupy floor space. Activated parameters describe selected work; total resident parameters and tensor bytes describe weight storage. Do not buy GPU memory based only on the activated count. kimi-k3-CARD glm-5.3-flash-CARD

Four kinds of memory

Weights are the learned constants. KV cache stores attention information associated with previous tokens. Recurrent state is the running summary used by linear-attention blocks. Temporary memory includes activations, graph capture, communication buffers, quantization/repacking workspaces and speculative verification. A model can fit its weights and still fail to start or run long requests.

A notebook is a useful analogy for recurrent state, but it is not a lossless transcript. Full-attention or sparse-history layers remain important. Conversely, a small recurrent state can need several snapshots per request for prefix caching, overlap and speculative rollback. The per-sequence state matrix alone is not the complete state-pool allocation. qwen3.8-27b-GR kimi-k3-GR glm-5.3-flash-GR

What precision changes

BF16 uses 16 bits per represented element. FP8 usually uses eight. NVFP4 and MXFP4 use four-bit values plus different scaling structures. Think of recording measurements to different precision, with scale notes attached to groups of values. They are not interchangeable file extensions. Exported checkpoints frequently mix several precisions. NV4 TE CUDNN

What the evidence labels mean

Publisher fact means that a primary source states it. Config extraction means the value is present in the cited machine-readable configuration. Source inspected means particular code ranges were read at a named commit. Publisher benchmark means the author measured it under stated conditions. Derived means a calculation here uses explicit assumptions. Candidate means a setting needs testing. Not verified means no confirmation was obtained; it does not mean false.

Reading paths

For understanding, read the comparison, the precision primer and the glossary. For deployment, read hardware, engine decisions, profiles and benchmark protocol. For auditability, read the evidence register and limitations.

For hands-on investigation, follow the experiment roadmap: qualify an exact build, check quality and state correctness, measure the workload, then introduce speculation, parallelism or KServe. The engine experiment pages explain which recent changes motivate each test and what would count as a useful result.

reports/comparison.md

Cross-model comparison

Architecture and deployment implications

ModelArchitecture identity and parameter scopeConfigured positional limitFirst engine/topology decision
Kimi K3Rounded 2.8T total / 104B active; hybrid KDA + gated MLA; latent MoE1,048,576SGLang initial candidate for NVIDIA mixed NVFP4; compare vLLM and SGLang for native MXFP4
GLM 5.3Approximately 743.38B main + 9.95B serialized MTP; 39B active headline; MLA with DSA; native FP8 packaging1,048,576vLLM FP8 and RedHat/Inferact NVFP4 first; SGLang challenger
GLM 5.3 FlashRounded 320B / 18B active; 321.323B Hub count; KDA + NoPE sparse MLA + mHC; multimodal1,048,576SGLang Blackwell FP8 initial candidate; compare vLLM FP8 and NVFP4
DeepSeek V4 Flash 0731Exact count unresolved; compressed/sparse attention, mHC, built-in DSpark1,048,576vLLM TP1/DP4+EP baseline and SGLang documented TP4 comparator; qualify eight-GPU adaptations
Qwen 3.8 27BRounded 27B dense language model; Gated DeltaNet + gated full attention; multimodal262,144vLLM TP1 BF16/FP8 and SGLang TP1; evaluate NVFP4 at equal quality
Qwen 3.8 Flash NextApproximately 125B main + 51B n-gram + 4B MTP, vision not separately counted; 6B active main; GDN + QSA262,144vLLM TP8 baseline for the NVIDIA mixed export; SGLang documented TP1 candidate, unverified on B300
Gemma 4 31B ITRounded 31B dense multimodal model; 5 sliding layers per global-attention layer262,144vLLM TP1 BF16/FP8/NVFP4 comparison; SGLang challenger with export-level probe
Mistral Medium 3.5Rounded 128B dense model; 88 language layers, GQA, Pixtral vision; static FP8 export262,144vLLM publisher TP8 baseline; compare TP2/4 and publisher-documented SGLang at equal workload

Each model links to its report and exact artifact evidence. Counts use different scopes and are not a leaderboard. GLM 5.3’s complete checkpoint count is about 753.33B including MTP. DeepSeek’s vLLM recipe gives a 284B/13B family headline while SGLang’s 0731 cookbook says 304B/13B; their accounting has not been reconciled into an exact target-plus-draft census. Positional limits are configuration values, sometimes including RoPE scaling, and do not establish training context, serving-image support or long-context quality. None of the topology recommendations is a local B300 measurement.

The important distinctions

Kimi is the capacity-heavy latent-MoE hybrid. GLM 5.3 uses sparse MLA without inheriting Flash’s recurrent architecture. GLM Flash combines KDA, sparse MLA, mHC and vision. DeepSeek 0731 has compressed/sparse attention and a bundled DSpark path. Qwen 27B is dense but hybrid. Flash Next adds a major n-gram table and differently quantized auxiliary path. Gemma is dense with heterogeneous local/global attention. Mistral is dense GQA with a full-history cache. These are different memory and scheduling problems, not eight names for the same Transformer.

Primary extraction sources: kimi-k3-CFG glm-5.3-CFG glm-5.3-flash-CFG deepseek-v4-flash-0731-CFG qwen3.8-27b-CFG qwen3.8-flash-next-CFG gemma-4-31b-it-CFG mistral-medium-3.5-CFG

There is no defensible universal winner yet

A useful comparison fixes the task distribution, prompt/output lengths, quality threshold, reasoning settings and latency objective. Compare per-node goodput for capacity planning and per-request latency for interactivity. Do not rank engines by unrelated benchmark rows from B200, GB300, RTX Blackwell and an unspecified B300 OEM node.

Choose a starting engine using evidence of the exact checkpoint path and the team’s operational constraints. Promote a candidate only after correctness and workload tests. vLLM and SGLang can both be benchmarked without changing the KServe lifecycle design for a basic single-node service; advanced disaggregation and routing require engine-specific integrations. KSRT KSLLM

Suggested workload portfolios

For short interactive chat, prioritize first-content latency and stable output pacing; include target-only baselines because drafting is not free. For coding agents, preserve multi-turn reasoning/tool serialization and measure actual draft acceptance. For long documents, constrain context and concurrency explicitly and measure retrieval quality. For multimodal tasks, include image preprocessing/encoder time and enforce input limits. For high-volume endpoints, compare independent small-model replicas against wider TP groups at equal node occupancy.

Benchmark protocol defines a reproducible experiment rather than claiming an unmeasured result.

hardware/B300-8GPU.md

Hardware target: eight B300 GPUs per node

Verified hardware facts versus supplied inventory

NVIDIA’s specifications, rechecked October 15, 2026 UTC, list a DGX B300 with eight B300 GPUs, 288 GB per GPU and approximately 2.3 TB aggregate GPU memory. Its CUDA GPU table identifies B300 as compute capability 10.3. Those are architectural references; the supplied SXM6-AC label does not identify a complete DGX-equivalent bill of materials. CPU, RAM, NVMe, NICs, firmware, power and cooling remain OEM-specific until inventoried. HW CC

Eight times 288 is 2,304 advertised GB. If those units are interpreted as decimal bytes, that is approximately 2,145.77 GiB. Treat this as a unit-conversion illustration, not a prediction of nvidia-smi output: query the memory actually exposed by the driver and allocator. Neither advertised total nor driver-visible total is all available for weights.

Preflight before tuning

Run python scripts/preflight.py --output preflight.json on the actual inference host. It runs read-only version and topology queries; it does not set GPU clocks, power limits, MIG mode, network devices or kernel parameters. Confirm eight intended devices, compute capability, driver/CUDA compatibility, peer-access topology and process exclusivity. Record CPU architecture so x86_64 B300 and Grace-based GB300 software images are not confused.

Do not treat a workstation RTX Blackwell measurement as an HGX B300 measurement. SM12x devices, B300 SM10.3, and B200 SM10.0 have different kernel and topology requirements. Likewise, GB300’s host and memory architecture is not an automatic match for an x86 SXM node. CC qwen3.8-27b-GR deepseek-v4-flash-0731-VR

Capacity plan

Use this budget for each GPU:

weights and repacked weights
+ persistent attention/index caches
+ recurrent-state pools and snapshots
+ temporary prefill/decode activations
+ communication and graph buffers
+ explicit safety headroom
<= memory exposed to the process

Shard each term only across the process group that actually shards it. TP can shard heads or linears; EP distributes experts; DP may replicate attention; DCP partitions decode context. None is a universal divisor for the entire memory budget. kimi-k3-GR glm-5.3-flash-GR qwen3.8-27b-GR

Recommended first node layouts

Workload familyFirst comparisonWhy this is a candidate, not a universal best
Kimi K3One 8-GPU targetWeight footprint rules out casual per-GPU replication
GLM 5.3 FP8TP8Conservative capacity and supported sharding baseline
GLM 5.3 NVFP4TP4 versus TP8May permit two replicas while retaining useful cache
GLM FlashTwo independent TP4 groups versus TP8Tests node goodput against per-request cooperation
DeepSeek 0731vLLM TP1/DP4 with EP, plus documented SGLang TP4; then eight-GPU experimentsDistinguishes published four-GPU configurations from local scaling hypotheses
Qwen 27B / Gemma 31BTP1 replicas versus TP2/4Small enough to evaluate communication-free replicas
Qwen Flash NextNVIDIA/vLLM TP8 baseline; documented SGLang TP1 candidate; TP4 capacity probeThe SGLang B300 recipe is unverified; n-gram and auxiliary precision affect capacity
Mistral MediumPublisher TP8 baseline, then TP2/4 comparisons; TP1 capacity probeDense compute and full-history KV change the trade-off

These combine publisher configurations and local experiments, as labeled; none is a measured B300 performance ranking here. The GLM 5.3 full checkpoint includes about 9.95B MTP parameters beyond its approximately 743.38B main model. Budget the modules actually loaded by the chosen target-only or speculative path. See comparison and memory accounting for exact artifacts and evidence.

Blackwell-specific optimization order

First establish an image that loads the correct artifact with finite, coherent outputs. Next verify kernel selection and mixed-format behavior. Only then compare FP8 versus NVFP4, attention backends, CUDA graph modes, batch caps, cache precision and speculative decoding. A newer kernel that is incompatible with block scales, SiTU or state replay can return incorrect results without an obvious crash. The Kimi fixes provide a concrete example. KIMIPR KIMIVPR GQUANT

Inspect whether time is spent in expert GEMMs, attention, routing, all-to-all, reductions, host scheduling, tokenization or memory movement. Changing a precision format cannot remove an unrelated queueing or network bottleneck. For low-concurrency dense models, launch and synchronization overhead can dominate; at longer contexts, cache bandwidth can dominate. This is a workload hypothesis to test with traces.

CPU, storage, network and power

Use local immutable checkpoint staging where operationally possible. Cold starts include downloading, deserialization, repacking, compilation and graph capture; do not report them as steady-state decoding. Mistral’s two weight layouts must not both be staged by accident. K3 can require terabyte-scale storage and loading headroom. mistral-medium-3.5-TREE kimi-k3-NV

For multi-node or prefill/decode transfer, inventory NIC-to-GPU affinity, link type, effective bandwidth, congestion and routing. NVLink within a node is not evidence of equivalent bandwidth between nodes. A separated prefill/decode layout should transfer every required token and recurrent-state component with compatible layouts. glm-5.3-flash-VR KSMULTI KSLLM

Do not use NVIDIA’s DGX system power specification as the measured power of this OEM node. Record wall power or a documented telemetry scope during the experiment. Performance per watt and cost per million successful tokens are derived metrics that need actual energy and pricing inputs; this atlas invents neither.

reports/memory-math.md

Memory mathematics, with boundaries

Uniform weight payload illustrations

For P parameters represented uniformly at b bits, the ideal payload is P × b / 8 bytes. These columns exclude scales, alignment, mixed precision, any modules outside the stated count, and runtime memory. Rounded publisher headlines are approximate accounting inputs, not exact tensor counts or strict memory lower bounds. All table sizes use decimal GB.

ModelAccounting basisUniform BF16 GBUniform FP8 GBUniform raw 4-bit GB
Kimi K3Rounded 2800B headline5,600.02,800.01,400.0
GLM 5.3753.32994048B checkpoint, including serialized MTP1,506.7753.3376.7
GLM 5.3 Flash321.32303139B Hub metadata count642.6321.3160.7
DeepSeek V4 Flash 0731Exact target-plus-draft count unresolved———
Qwen 3.8 27BRounded 27B language-model headline54.027.013.5
Qwen 3.8 Flash NextRounded 180B main/n-gram/MTP subtotal360.0180.090.0
Gemma 4 31B ITRounded 31B headline62.031.015.5
Mistral Medium 3.5Rounded 128B headline256.0128.064.0

The catalog distinguishes these scopes. GLM 5.3’s 753,329,940,480 reported parameters include 9,952,920,576 MTP parameters identified from the pinned BF16 shard headers, leaving 743,377,019,904 for the main model. Its familiar 743B headline therefore cannot describe the whole checkpoint. Flash’s 320B publisher headline is rounded; the Hub metadata reports 321,323,031,390. These metadata counts and derived illustrations are not measured GPU allocations. glm-5.3-META GLM parameter accounting glm-5.3-flash-META

Qwen Flash Next’s 125B + 51B + 4B subtotal covers the publisher’s main/n-gram/MTP quantities; it does not separately count vision. DeepSeek’s vLLM recipe uses a 284B family headline while SGLang’s 0731 cookbook says 304B. The different scopes have not been reconciled into an exact tensor census, so the table leaves that row unset. The hypothetical precision columns do not assert that those artifact variants exist. Qwen accounting DeepSeek accounting

Scale overhead

For an ideal one-dimensional payload, MXFP4 with one byte of scale per 32 four-bit values consumes 0.5 + 1/32 = 0.53125 bytes per value. NVFP4 with one byte per 16 values consumes 0.5625 bytes per value before the outer tensor scale. These correspond to 4.25 and 4.5 effective bits for those parts. Real exports can add padding, layouts and mixed modules. The Transformer Engine training recipe also describes 16×16 weight scaling; the one-dimensional estimate here must not be applied to every NVFP4 recipe. NV4 TE4 CUDNN

For Qwen Flash Next, the approximately 51B n-gram table is FP8 in the NVIDIA derivative, so its ideal value payload is around 51 GB before scales and padding. Four-bit estimates that omit that table are incomplete. qwen3.8-flash-next-NV

Uniform GQA cache

For an unpadded, non-shared cache with separate K and V:

KV bytes = 2 × layers × KV_heads × head_dim
           × tokens_per_sequence × concurrent_sequences × bytes_per_element

Use it only where those assumptions hold. At 262,144 tokens and one sequence, Mistral’s 2 × 88 × 8 × 128 × 262144 × 2 gives 88 GiB. Qwen 27B’s 2 × 16 × 4 × 256 × 262144 × 2 gives 16 GiB for its full-attention layers, plus separate linear state. These are aggregate, unsharded BF16 payloads; the engine determines per-rank allocation and any replication. A factor-of-two precision change affects element payload, not necessarily every allocation. mistral-medium-3.5-CFG qwen3.8-27b-CFG

Do not apply this formula unchanged to Kimi MLA, DeepSeek compressed attention, GLM sparse indexers or Gemma’s heterogeneous local/global and key/value-sharing behavior. A head count in config is not an allocator specification. kimi-k3-CFG deepseek-v4-flash-0731-CFG gemma-4-31b-it-CFG

Recurrent state

A simple per-sequence matrix-state illustration is:

matrix bytes = linear_layers × value_heads × key_dim × value_dim × state_bytes

Under that layout assumption, with FP32 state and one sequence:

ModelLinear layers × value heads × key dimension × value dimension × bytesMatrix payload
Kimi K369 × 96 × 128 × 128 × 4414 MiB
GLM 5.3 Flash34 × 64 × 128 × 128 × 4136 MiB
Qwen 3.8 27B48 × 48 × 128 × 128 × 4144 MiB
Qwen 3.8 Flash Next36 × 48 × 128 × 128 × 4108 MiB

These are aggregate matrix-only illustrations before sharding; they exclude convolution, padding and extra slots. The published Qwen cookbook’s fuller state-slot estimate is therefore larger. kimi-k3-CFG glm-5.3-flash-CFG qwen3.8-27b-CFG qwen3.8-flash-next-CFG qwen3.8-27b-GR

A state pool may reserve multiple slots per running request for prefix checkpoints, overlapping work and draft verification. The SGLang Qwen recipe distinguishes ordinary state slots from speculative intermediate buffers and replay-ring implementations. Use actual slot counts and boot logs; never multiply a generic “default concurrency” by only one matrix. qwen3.8-27b-GR

Practical capacity workflow

Measure post-load free memory, graph-capture peak, KV token capacity, state-slot capacity and maximum running requests separately. Increase request length with batch fixed, then batch with length fixed. Repeat after changing speculation, cache dtype or multimodal input shape. Leave a documented safety margin and test cancellation/eviction paths under load.

Run python scripts/memory.py --help for the tested analytic calculator. It refuses to present a universal hybrid-cache estimate and labels all numbers as payload estimates.

reports/precision-and-kernels.md

Precision, serialization and kernels

A precision name is not a deployment contract

TermMeaningCommon mistake
BF1616-bit floating representation with broad exponent rangeCalling dequantized FP4 weights the original BF16 model
FP8Eight-bit floating family, often E4M3 for inferenceIgnoring block, channel, tensor and dynamic-activation schemes
MXFP8Microscaled FP8 with shared block scaleTreating it as the same packing as ordinary FP8
MXFP4Four-bit values with microscaling, typically 32-value blocksRenaming it NVFP4
NVFP4E2M1 values, commonly one E4M3 scale per 16 values plus an outer FP32 scaleAssuming all tensors or all recipes use the same precision and scale layout
W4A4Four-bit weights and activations for the affected operatorsAssuming routers, KV cache and vision are also four-bit
W4A16Four-bit weights with higher-precision activationsExpecting the same compute path as W4A4
Weight-only FP8Weights compressed; activation treatment is loader/kernel-specificAssuming its exporter label fixes runtime activation precision

The representations and scale distinctions are documented by NVIDIA. E2M1, E4M3 and E8M0 refer to exponent/mantissa layouts, not model sizes. NV4 TE TE4 CUDNN

Four layers of compatibility

Numeric format: bit layout, scale data types and granularity. Serialized representation: tensor names, packed shapes, exclusions, exporter metadata. Mathematical operator: activation, bias, normalization, router weighting, clamping and residual behavior. Runtime implementation: supported kernel, alignment, repacking, sharding and target GPU architecture.

A failure at any layer can break the result. In the inspected SGLang code, FP4 GEMM selects different W4A4 and W4A16 paths and checks whether FlashInfer functionality is available. Padding rules differ across kernel families. In the Kimi fixes, correct scale loading and SiTU activation behavior are as important as selecting four-bit matrix multiplication. GQUANT KIMIPR KIMIVPR

Exporter differences in this catalog

Kimi’s native routed-expert MXFP4/MXFP8 scheme is distinct from NVIDIA’s mixed NVFP4/FP8 export; neither description means that every tensor or runtime activation uses that format. RedHat and Inferact GLM 5.3 quantize main routed experts to NVFP4 while retaining MTP precision. RedHat GLM Flash instead combines main routed-expert NVFP4 with FP8 MTP experts. Kimi precision GLM precision GLM Flash precision

NVIDIA Qwen 27B mixes NVFP4 MLP/head with FP8 attention. Its card and quantization metadata disagree on the ModelOpt version; preserve the actual files and record that discrepancy. NVIDIA Flash Next retains BF16 components and an FP8 n-gram table. RedHat Gemma excludes vision, embeddings and head from Transformer-block quantization. Mistral uses static-activation FP8. Each linked precision report records the exact artifacts and primary sources; a single NVFP4 loader flag cannot express all these contracts. Qwen 27B precision Flash Next precision Gemma precision Mistral precision

FP8 KV is independent

Weight precision changes model operations. KV precision changes stored attention history. Recurrent-state precision changes an additional state representation. Test these separately: baseline weights + baseline cache; low-precision weights + unchanged cache; then lower-precision cache/state. Otherwise an accuracy difference cannot be assigned to its cause.

The SGLang Qwen recipe gives a concrete metadata trap: some derivative exports default auto KV to FP8, whereas NVIDIA’s export leaves it BF16 without an explicit setting. Matching command text with auto therefore does not necessarily match effective precision. qwen3.8-27b-GR

Why four-bit can win, and why it can lose

A lower-byte representation may reduce weight bandwidth and let a larger useful batch or cache fit. It can also introduce scale handling, conversion, padding, calibration error and backend constraints. At low concurrency, synchronization and launch overhead may dominate the saved arithmetic. At long context, attention-history traffic can dominate expert weights. These are reasons to measure, not reasons to reject quantization.

The decision metric is successful, quality-accepted work under a latency objective. A faster kernel with a regression in tool arguments or retrieval is not a production improvement. Publisher results can establish plausibility, but they do not certify your prompts, image distribution, sampling settings or software bill of materials.

Calibration and quality gates

Use a representative holdout with coding, tool use, instruction following, multilingual questions, long context and vision where supported. Freeze the tokenizer, chat serialization and reasoning effort. Include repeated seeds for stochastic tests and paired comparisons. Report absolute differences, sample sizes and uncertainty rather than only a rounded “recovery” percentage.

A native quantization-aware-trained release and a post-training quantized derivative are not the same intervention. Do not attribute the former’s quality to an exporter conversion that was never evaluated. kimi-k3-CARD qwen3.8-27b-NV glm-5.3-NV

Back to contents ↑
frameworks/DECISION.md

vLLM, SGLang and KServe: justified roles

Decision

Use vLLM and SGLang as competing execution engines. Use KServe as the optional Kubernetes lifecycle layer. Begin with a single-node colocated prefill/decode service. Select an engine per exact artifact, not by brand loyalty. Introduce disaggregation when measured queueing and transfer economics justify it. KS KSRT KSLLM

Why vLLM is a strong first path here

The inspected registry resolves model classes to implementation modules, including vllm.models.* namespaces for several newer architectures. Its Kimi DSpark implementation exposes model-specific MLA, draft quantization, hidden-state projection and fused reductions. Official recipes cover the requested families, and Mistral recommends vLLM while also documenting SGLang. These are compatibility and reproducibility reasons, not measured engine rankings. VREG VDS mistral-medium-3.5-CARD

The exact NVIDIA Flash Next mixed export has a vLLM TP8 baseline. Its MTP loader fix is merged and belongs to the v0.31.0 source history. Kimi’s separate FP8-attention PR remains open, while NVIDIA documents a patched vLLM container for that export. Readiness therefore depends on the exact source and image, not just the engine name. qwen3.8-flash-next-NV QPR VREL-QWEN-ANCESTRY KIMIVPR kimi-k3-NV

Why SGLang is a strong first path here

Its model-specific cookbooks make recurrent-state allocation, cache hierarchy and speculative strategy explicit. The current release includes hybrid-model and Kimi correctness/performance work. The Kimi mixed NVFP4/FP8 compatibility fix is merged, and its source handles multiple quantized GEMM pathways. These justify a first compatibility trial for Kimi NVFP4 and a serious comparison for GLM Flash and Qwen hybrid models. They do not substitute for B300 workload measurements. GREL GQUANT KIMIPR glm-5.3-flash-GR qwen3.8-27b-GR

SGLang also has exact-artifact evidence for DeepSeek 0731 TP4 and NVIDIA Flash Next NVFP4 TP1. The latter’s B300 recipe cell is explicitly marked verified: false, and its PR reports no new GPU measurements. Documented configurations remain candidates until the target hardware and workload are tested. deepseek-v4-flash-0731-CARD qwen3.8-flash-next-GPR qwen3.8-flash-next-GR-SOURCE

The inspected SGLang registry can discover an architecture through package entry classes, log an import error, or append a Transformers fallback during normalization. This makes the resolved implementation and import logs important evidence. A registry match alone is weaker than a successful exact-artifact load with verified kernels. GREG

What KServe contributes

KServe adds declarative deployment, lifecycle reconciliation and integration with Kubernetes resources. Its LLMInferenceService path adds composable LLM configuration, specialized scheduling, distributed workloads and prefill/decode orchestration. This is a control-plane choice, not an FP4 arithmetic choice. KS KSLLM KSCFG

For this eight-GPU single-node task, a custom-container InferenceService is a transparent initial path for either engine. For advanced vLLM/llm-d routing and transfer, use the compatible LLMInferenceService configuration rather than pretending that replacing a container name certifies SGLang integration. A dedicated SGLang-aware orchestration design must explicitly preserve its transfer and cache semantics.

The refreshed KServe source review separates stable capabilities from newer master templates and controller changes. An SGLang runtime template, where present, establishes a particular integration path; prefill/decode transfer, routing metrics and scaling still need their own version-specific qualification.

Decision matrix

RequirementPreferred initial routePromotion condition
Exact Kimi NVIDIA NVFP4 compatibilitySGLang with merged mixed-format pathLoad, SiTU/scale checks, target-only quality
Exact NVIDIA Flash Next NVFP4vLLM TP8 baseline; SGLang TP1 documented candidateCorrect mixed MTP/PLE handling and B300 validation in the installed image
Mistral producer alignmentvLLM TP8 baseline; SGLang documented comparatorCorrected config, tokenizer and vision validated
Qwen/Gemma smaller-model fleetEither, TP1 baselineBetter node goodput at equal quality/latency
Hybrid pool tuningBenchmark bothBoot-log accounting and cancellation correctness
Basic Kubernetes serviceCustom-container InferenceServiceServer-side schema dry run and readiness test
Advanced prefill/decode or cluster routingVersion-matched LLM orchestrationCorrect state transfer and measured crossover

Every recommendation is conditional. Actual measurements are intentionally absent until run on the target hardware.

Turn the choice into an experiment

Use the shared roadmap to compare stable and pinned candidate builds, then compare qualified vLLM/SGLang configurations under the same application objective. The detailed vLLM and SGLang plans connect recent source changes to expected mechanisms, controlled variables and reasons to reject a candidate. Apply the KServe plan after the direct-engine baseline, so routing and lifecycle effects remain identifiable.

A newer nightly can contain a relevant correctness fix and still regress another workload. A kernel implementation can exist before a model calls it. A model-specific improvement can exclude the atlas's exact export or speculative method. The experiment record must establish those prerequisites before treating a release note as an available optimization.

Recent changes, mechanism and limits

These changes address different bottlenecks. A faster metadata kernel helps only when that kernel is on the active path and material in the trace. A scheduler change matters when admission is constrained. A cheaper proposal head helps only if accepted output saves more target work than the extra drafting costs. These are hypotheses to measure, not additive speedup percentages.

A material documentation correction: #61044's description still places the handle allocation after init_snapshot. Its merged implementation instead performs a tiny matrix multiplication and biased linear operation in init_device, before the snapshot, treating handle memory as part of the baseline. Use the merged code when interpreting memory numbers. VEXP-DIFF-61044

The MegaMoE NVFP4 path also keeps its L2 activation scale as a Python float. The upstream review identifies graph replay after a weight reload that changes this scale as unfinished work. A future model caller must qualify reload plus graph replay separately; the new row cannot justify enabling it for all NVFP4 exports. VEXP-61538 VEXP-SRC-MEGA

The earlier source review inspected registry.py lines 1–350 and Kimi dspark_mla.py lines 1–190 at f8460ef…; it also listed the Kimi NVIDIA directory. The registry maps several recent architectures into vllm.models.*. The draft MLA constructs non-causal multi-token attention, a draft quantization configuration and target-feature projection, with reductions that can be fused. These observations motivate tracing the exact loader/backend path before changing TP, draft dtype or fusion. Directory contents alone do not demonstrate runtime support. VREG VDS VDIR

Cache, graphs, distribution and restart

Prefix reuse has to preserve each participating cache/state group. A recurrent checkpoint represents a particular processed prefix; sharing attention blocks without the matching recurrent state is insufficient. v0.31.0 renamed the shared-checkpoint option to --enable-mamba-shared-prefix-checkpoint. The hybrid-manager design page explicitly describes an older source basis, so use it for the mechanism and inspect the selected engine for current grouping/support. V03 stresses the boundaries where reused state becomes visible. VEXP-REL VEXP-HYBRID

CUDA graphs reuse captured GPU work to reduce launch overhead. Graph selection depends on batch shape and backend support; the requested mode can be downgraded. More capture shapes can require more memory and initialization time. Record the resolved mode and replay path. --enforce-eager also disables JIT warmup in v0.31.0, so comparing that switch alone does not isolate graph replay. V04 keeps compilation and warmup fixed for its graph-only comparison. VEXP-GRAPH VEXP-REL

Expert parallelism distributes experts across the TP×DP ranks while attention uses its own replication/sharding pattern. Its communication and kernel backend must match the actual node and dependencies. Prefill/decode separation instead assigns phases to different engines and transfers state; it can help tail latency while introducing transfer and duplicated-residency costs. These are separate decisions. V08 compares whole-node allocations, then V09 asks whether a 4+4 split earns its overhead. VEXP-EP VEXP-PD

The weight-cache daemon retains post-processed GPU weights and exposes them through local IPC. Matching fingerprints, quantization support and a surviving daemon are required; a mismatch can fall back to disk loading. This reduces one part of a restart, while compilation, graph capture and service readiness still need timing. Initialized-engine snapshots are a different experimental mechanism: current documentation limits them to one GPU/TP1, no speculation, the same host/software environment, and reports validation only for dense FP16. Neither mechanism establishes that an eight-GPU KServe service can restore all request state. VEXP-PRELOAD VEXP-SNAPSHOT

Kimi compatibility remains a separate gate

Fresh retrieval still shows #52406 open. It handles ModelOpt FP8-block projection padding/trimming and MLA scale absorption for Kimi attention. Its author separates unit/static validation of that branch from an eight-B300 evaluation using equivalent compatibility logic. The newly merged MegaMoE work does not resolve this attention-layout issue. VEXP-52406

NVIDIA's pinned Kimi NVFP4 card documents a patched vLLM container and bundled runtime patches. Preserve that as a distinct artifact candidate. An unmodified stable image, a main build and the publisher's patched image need separate identities and load/quality gates. kimi-k3-NV

Before adopting any candidate, trace model resolution → weight loader → quantization/backend dispatch → scheduler/cache → draft verification → output parser. The experiment plan specifies the controls and failure cases. No independent end-to-end B300 reproduction was established by this source review.

Released source, main and published artifacts

The earlier atlas inspection at 877be2974aa151a984e52822c028276c2b1e9303 covered the registry and ModelOpt quantization lines 1–240. This expansion adds a separate, broader snapshot. A nightly can lag a merge, and dependency changes can alter behavior independently of SGLang's commit.

Merge time does not prove release inclusion. Qwen NEXTN graph-input fusion, PR #41175, merged October 2 at 00:17 UTC, before v0.5.21's publication. Nevertheless, the release's pinned eagle.py lacks prepare_verify_commit_outputs and prepare_draft_extend_inputs; current main contains them. The comparison shows separate release-branch commits. Treat these functions as a main-source candidate for the reviewed release comparison. GEXP-PR41175, GEXP-STABLE-QWENPREP, GEXP-MAIN-QWENPREP, GEXP-PR41175-COMPARE.

Hybrid state and prefix reuse

A hybrid request needs attention KV and recurrent/convolution state representing the same prefix. Branches, overlapping execution and speculative verification can require several state slots per request. Giving nearly all post-weight HBM to KV can leave free token pages but too few state slots to admit work. The useful split depends on request length, cache strategy and speculation. Record the resolved strategy and pool capacities; a generic default and a model-specific recipe need not resolve identically. GEXP-QWEN, GEXP-GLMFLASH.

ReplaySSM uses a replay ring instead of ordinary per-request speculative intermediate-state slots. PR #40517 made its KDA gate capability-based, allowing GLM-5.3 Flash rather than depending on a Kimi-specific config class; v0.5.21 lists the change. This motivates memory and rollback comparisons, without establishing a latency win. GEXP-PR40517, GEXP-REL.

The int8 checkpoint pool is already present in stable source. It compresses idle radix-owned temporal state, retains convolution windows at their native dtype and restores checkpoints into active slots. This differs from reducing active-state arithmetic precision. Scales, convolution state and the additional pool still consume memory. Source comments describing quality do not replace long-conversation evaluation: rounding at a cache hit changes the starting state for later computation. GEXP-STABLE-MAMBA, GEXP-SRC-MAMBA.

The unified radix cache groups full-attention, sliding-window and recurrent components behind a tree core. Rust is a v0.5.21 feature, but current source can resolve even an explicit Rust request to Python for unsupported components, session-aware caching, platform or installation constraints. Check the logged concrete core before comparing treatments. GEXP-PR39627, GEXP-SRC-TREECORE, GEXP-SRC-CACHE.

HiCache extends reuse to host memory and optionally storage. L2 belongs to one engine instance; independent replicas do not automatically share it. L3 supports cross-instance reuse only through an appropriately configured backend and namespace. Hits can save prefill work but add transfer time, CPU memory demand and storage traffic. Compare cold, GPU-hit, host-hit and storage-hit cases separately, then replay the real mixture. GEXP-HICACHE.

Main PR #44173 adds an opt-in buffer_only optimization: an SWA component backs up the trailing window required at a node endpoint. Source trims transfer rows, storage keys and dependent sidecars consistently. This can reduce SWA writes, not every component's memory. Validate node splits, hits inside nodes, unequal windows and storage misses before adoption. GEXP-PR44173, GEXP-SRC-SWA.

Precision must reach the intended implementation

The model registry logs and skips non-strict import failures, and can normalize an unresolved architecture to a Transformers fallback. Capture the resolved class and warnings; accepting a model name does not establish its optimized path. GEXP-SRC-REG.

ModelOpt dispatches modules through a quantization map. Current source recognizes both FP8_PB_WO and FP8_BLOCK_SCALES for linears and fused experts, derives their block size and rejects conflicting sizes. Released PR #38726 addresses the exact NVIDIA Qwen Flash Next case in which metadata copies name MTP experts differently. An NVFP4-labeled checkpoint can still require FP8 expert, attention and embedding handling. Record loaded module methods and exclusions for the exact export. GEXP-PR38726, GEXP-SRC-QUANT.

The earlier Kimi PR #35077 is included in v0.5.21 source. Its author reported correctness on eight GB300 GPUs and no speed test. That supports a B300 compatibility candidate, not measured B300 throughput. KIMIPR, GREL-KIMI-ANCESTRY.

GEXP-PR41175, GEXP-PR43604, GEXP-PR44557, GEXP-PR40498, GEXP-PR36810. The pinned code ranges below substantiate the mechanism descriptions.

Prefill/decode separation adds a transfer boundary and consumes GPUs in both roles. GLM Flash's mechanical preview uses TP2 prefill + TP2 decode on four GB300 GPUs, dummy weights, NIXL, BF16 KV, TileLang DSA and Triton MoE. It lacks real-weight load/accuracy qualification; the Playground's Mooncake selection is a separate path. G09's 4+4 layout is an atlas scaling experiment. Keep speculation and prefill CP disabled, equal TP sizes and the preview's backend combination during initial qualification; its flashinfer_trtllm PD path is untested. Preserve every required state component and layout. A published layout using more than eight GPUs does not fit this node's comparison budget. GEXP-GLMFLASH, GEXP-PD, GEXP-KIMI.

PR #44093 changes GLM Flash's fallback expert-count default; the author explains that real checkpoints already override it. It provides no reason to expect an improvement for the pinned atlas checkpoint. GEXP-PR44093.

PR #40965 remained open. Its observed patch head, 9f9415c5072aaf89f3359c238649eac7bc85acc7, proposes preserving prior-turn thinking in the field consumed by relevant Jinja templates when adapting Anthropic Messages requests. This is the optional G12 API-compatibility probe. An ordinary nightly must not be described as including this unmerged patch. GEXP-PR40965, GEXP-PR40965-PATCH.

frameworks/kserve/ARCHITECTURE.md

KServe deployment architecture

KServe manages the Kubernetes service lifecycle. vLLM or SGLang still loads the checkpoint, schedules token work, owns the caches and executes GPU kernels. Qualify the exact engine/checkpoint combination directly, then measure what the platform adds. The companion experiments turn the deployment choices below into comparisons with explicit gates; none has been run on a cluster for this atlas.

Release, branch and development image are separate observations

The fresh check for the October 16 local-date expansion, retrieved October 15, 2026 UTC, still resolves the latest stable release to v0.21.0, published September 25. Its tag is d1482554fc4f66dd41aee70e01f5174e24f265bd. The separately fetched master is 22be2b0496eea4548a98395c3abccf48d8235385, committed October 15 at 19:47:34 UTC. Selected schemas, presets and controller functions were inspected at both commits. KEXP-REL KEXP-MASTER

The public overview still identifies its version as 0.20. At v0.21.0, both LLMInferenceService and LLMInferenceServiceConfig serve v1alpha1 and v1alpha2; v1alpha2 is the storage version. The stable product release therefore still includes alpha-version LLM APIs. Use the chosen release's CRDs, conversion webhook and templates together; a website example is not a release-matched deployment receipt. KEXP-DOCS KEXP-CRD21 KEXP-CFGCRD21

KServe's inspected controller publisher maps master pushes to the mutable Docker tag kserve/llmisvc-controller:latest; it is a development-image path, not a separately established nightly qualification channel. Registry metadata reports that tag updated October 15 at 20:13:47 UTC, with index digest sha256:a9bd5f40cdff8c589d07ffd59e29337f9a1e7b2714cced7b0120acb5c714206f. No image was pulled, its source revision was not independently established, and it was not run. Record the platform-specific digest and build provenance before using it in an experiment. KEXP-PUBLISHMASTER KEXP-DEVIMAGE

Understand the two paths through the system

Control: desired service/config → KServe controller → workloads and routing resources

Request: client → gateway → selected engine replica → generated response stream
                           ↑
                   endpoint picker, when configured

P/D request: gateway/router → prefill role → compatible state transfer → decode role

The controller reconciles objects; the request path runs through gateway/router components and engine pods. An endpoint picker selects a replica; the engine's own scheduler selects and batches token work inside it. Gateway latency, replica selection and GPU scheduling need separate measurements. The LLM API represents main/decode workloads, optional prefill and worker workloads, and router configuration. KEXP-API21 KEXP-DOCS

The SGLang path is new evidence: its template and runtime definition are absent from v0.21.0, although the introducing master commit predates the stable publication date. Release-branch contents decide availability, not merge date alone. The master selector explicitly calls this a single-node non-P/D choice, and the bundled runtime image is lmsysorg/sglang:v0.5.14. Its presence does not qualify this atlas's newer SGLang images or its large models, nor does it supply an established SGLang P/D adapter. A subtle routing difference deserves an explicit check. v0.21.0 includes preset YAML using approximate-prefix and inflight-load producers, a prefix-affinity filter and token-load scoring. However, the composition code requires a declared router version of at least 0.11.0 to inject those presets; its shipped scheduler is 0.10.0. With shipped defaults and no explicit or previously preserved scheduler configuration, it uses the older schedulerConfigText() path, which includes queue, KV-utilization and prefix scoring. Master updates the router to 0.11.0. Capture the rendered endpoint-picker configuration: file existence, controller version and active routing policy are three different observations. KEXP-EPP21 KEXP-COMPOSE21 KEXP-EPPSERVICE21 KEXP-SCHEDULER21 KEXP-ROUTER

These changes are visible in fetched master commits and inspected source, not reproduced results. KEXP-DSMERGE KEXP-DSKEDA KEXP-DSROLLOUT KEXP-DSZERO KEXP-WVA KEXP-ROUTER

Master's DisaggregatedSet gate defaults off. Its P/D presets request the backend, so enabling the gate can move existing services when the required CRDs are present. The controller explicitly reports that moves onto or off the backend delete old workloads without waiting for replacement readiness. Test this as a potentially interrupting migration. For autoscaled roles, the implementation additionally requires the RoleScaler CRD; it reports a specific fallback reason when absent. KEXP-DSCONFIGMASTER KEXP-DSMASTER

Master rejects newly added or changed WVA configuration and reports existing WVA autoscaling as unsupported/disabled. It reconciles direct spec.scaling.keda, with a separate prefill scaling configuration where needed. An upgrade experiment must account for legacy scaling resources and current desired replicas before changing controllers; copying the website's WVA example into this branch is not a valid migration plan. KEXP-VALIDATIONMASTER KEXP-SCALINGMASTER

Design within the eight-GPU budget

One TP8 replica owns all eight GPUs. Eight TP1 replicas, two TP4 replicas and one TP8 replica have different memory, scheduling and failure behavior. Compare them only after each topology loads the same artifact correctly; report whole-node goodput.

P/D normally needs a model copy in each role. A checkpoint that only fits at TP8 cannot be made into TP4 prefill plus TP4 decode merely by changing Kubernetes fields. Two TP8 roles require sixteen GPUs under that placement assumption. For this node, begin with an artifact whose two independently qualified role allocations fit the budget, or record the larger-model P/D experiment as blocked on more hardware.

The same accounting applies to rollouts. With all eight GPUs occupied, a second TP8 replica cannot become ready until another eight-GPU group is available or the old replica stops. A zero-downtime objective therefore needs spare capacity or a separately qualified smaller replica topology. The stable LLM API exposes rolloutStrategy, but a setting cannot create physical capacity. KEXP-API21

Prove transfer and lifecycle behavior

The stable P/D templates launch vLLM and conditionally add producer/consumer NixlConnector configuration when NIXL is importable and no user transfer configuration overrides it. Their fallback logs that P/D KV transfer stays disabled. Healthy pods alone therefore do not establish a working transfer path. Record the actual connector, transport, transfer counters and prefill work performed at decode. For hybrid models, additionally establish compatible recurrent/conv state transfer; token-KV transport alone is insufficient. KEXP-DEC21 KEXP-PREFILL21 Benchmark protocol

Set readiness after model load and warmup, measure startup allowances, and test request draining before terminating a sharded group. Record queue/arrival signals and metric availability before enabling scaling. Zero engine replicas remove pod-local demand signals: a successful wake-up needs an observable trigger that remains available and an explicit policy for requests arriving during startup. K05 tests that design rather than assuming that a zero minimum provides request buffering.

Authenticate at the gateway, bound request sizes and token budgets, and define tenant/cache-key isolation. Pin model revisions and all participating images, retain the resolved configuration, and prepare a rollback artifact. These are experiment prerequisites and operating recommendations, not claims that every default template provides them.

Included rendering path

The renderer emits a custom-container InferenceService in Standard mode with an existing model PVC, explicit GPU limits, shared memory and probes. Supply a real digest, existing node labels and measured host resources. It neither deploys the service nor constructs the LLMInferenceService experiments above. Deployment boundary KCONST21

python deploy/kserve/render.py --help
python deploy/kserve/render.py \
  --profile profiles/qwen3.8-27b.bf16.vllm.baseline.json \
  --image registry.example/engine@sha256:YOUR_64_HEX_DIGEST \
  --pvc model-cache --model-subpath qwen/pinned-revision \
  --name qwen27b --cpu 16 --host-memory 128Gi > service.json
kubectl apply --dry-run=server -f service.json

The illustrative digest is deliberately rejected until replaced. CPU and memory values are examples, not measured requirements. The final command is an operator-side schema/admission check against a chosen cluster; it does not measure engine correctness, transfer, latency or availability.

Recent changes that justify targeted experiments

Primary changes, immutable source links and applicability details are in the expanded vLLM source review, SGLang source review and KServe architecture review. The corresponding vLLM, SGLang and KServe experiment cards define controls, measurements and rejection criteria.

Three traps exposed by source review

Merged before publication does not prove release inclusion. A release branch can omit a commit merged to main before its publication time. SGLang's Qwen graph-preparation change is a concrete example in the refreshed source review. Check the release's actual file or ancestry rather than comparing dates alone.

A kernel row does not prove a model uses it. The reviewed vLLM MegaMoE NVFP4 feature-matrix change adds a lower-level path without a model caller in that patch. It is useful future implementation evidence, not permission to invent a backend flag or claim that every NVFP4 checkpoint is faster. VEXP-61538.

A template does not prove it is active. KServe's endpoint-picker configuration depends on image/version selection. The stable default and master dependency stack can select different paths even when similarly named preset files exist. Inspect the rendered deployment and effective scheduler configuration; the KServe review gives the exact selection gate.

Upgrade procedure and promotion decision

Follow X01–X09 in the experiment roadmap. First qualify the exact artifact with target-only loading and representative quality checks. Compare baseline and candidate using identical effective precision, topology, serialized workload, cache state and objectives. Then isolate features within the candidate where supported. Include long context, cancellation, eviction, stop handling and graph replay before a speed claim; measure natural speculative acceptance and keep the target-only baseline.

Use the run-record template for immutable identities, dependency versions, effective flags, workload hashes, predeclared thresholds and results. Promote only after repeated measurements meet quality, latency, error and capacity requirements. A regression can justify keeping stable even when main contains a promising optimization. Preserve the previous artifact and rollback procedure.

No profile has been promoted to tested status by this update. No missing model support has been inferred from a neighboring architecture, and no engine was installed or launched.
