benchmarks/PROTOCOL.md

Benchmark and correctness protocol

Use the experiment roadmap to choose an ordered comparison, then the engine-specific vLLM, SGLang or KServe plan for current hypotheses. experiment.template.json records controls, artifact identities, predeclared thresholds and eventual evidence. It is a manual run-record template, not an executable profile or a completed result.

Define the decision before measuring

Choose a service-level objective and quality threshold. For example, compare request goodput while meeting a declared p95 first-token and generation-latency budget. Do not replace those budgets with convenient measured numbers after a run. Store the exact values in each experiment manifest; this atlas supplies no invented production SLA.

Compare engines on the same node, model revision, export, tokenizer, chat template, thinking mode, sampling, input/output lengths, concurrency, arrival process and cache state. A warm prefix hit against a cold-cache run is not a fair comparison. Weight precision, KV precision and recurrent-state precision must be recorded separately. Cap vision payloads and account for visual tokens rather than pretending an image is one text token.

Correctness gates before speed

First test target-only loading and finite outputs against the publisher/reference implementation. Cover ordinary chat, arithmetic, coding, multilingual requests, EOS/stop handling, JSON schemas, tool-call boundaries and image inputs where supported. Quantized checkpoints require a representative held-out quality evaluation, not only one coherent answer. Compare aggregate metrics and failure categories, not exact sampled strings.

Then test long contexts, repeated prefixes, cache eviction, cancellation, graph replay, batch size changes and partial failures. Hybrid models must preserve recurrent and convolutional state as well as token KV. For speculative decoding, verify that accepted and rejected branches commit or restore the right state. Use native acceptance; reject simulated-acceptance environment settings. Evaluate the same workloads with speculation disabled.

For 0731 DeepSeek use its encoding and DSpark contract, not a preview model's MTP invocation. For Kimi match the drafter to the engine and target. For Qwen Flash Next include PLE/ngram and draft tensors in load accounting. For Gemma verify no unsupported audio feature was accidentally enabled from a generic example.

Minimum experiment matrix

Run target-only first. Select feasible input lengths from 4K, 16K, 32K and 64K; the complete serialized input, including template and image tokens, plus requested output must fit both the configured context cap and model limit. Reduce or skip infeasible points. Raising a cap, including extension toward 128K or the native maximum, requires separate capacity and accuracy gates. Sweep output lengths representative of the application, such as 128 and 1,024 tokens, plus the real trace. Include concurrency 1, 8, 32 and the point just before saturation. Repeat independent runs, randomize order and retain failures.

For dense Qwen/Gemma compare TP1 replicas with TP2/4 as total-node capacity, not only one process's throughput. For large MoE compare the documented TP/DP/EP topologies before introducing cross-node communication. For hybrid models sweep the state/KV pool split only with measured pool demand. For DSpark/MTP record acceptance, verification work and queueing at each load, not a single best batch-one speedup.

Metric definitions

TTFT is time from request submission to first generated semantic content. State whether reasoning or tool deltas count. First-answer latency excludes hidden/reasoning content and may be much larger. End-to-end latency includes the complete response. ITL requires reliable per-token emission timing: an SSE chunk can contain zero or several tokens, so chunk gaps are not automatically token latency.

TPOT is generation time per output token under an explicitly stated formula. A network-based approximation from first content to final event divided by completion_tokens−1 is a proxy, not a GPU-only measurement. Output throughput uses the total reported output-token count divided by full measurement wall time. Goodput counts only successful requests/tokens meeting the declared latency and quality objective. Report error rate, p50/p95/p99 and offered load; never drop failures from the denominator silently.

Natural acceptance is observed accepted draft work under the deployed sampling procedure. Define whether the length includes the extra target token. Memory includes resident weights, caches, recurrent state, graph capture, activations, workspaces and communication buffers. Keep physical/advertised GB and binary GiB explicit.

Included runner and its boundaries

run_streaming.py provides a lightweight, closed-loop, concurrent HTTP/SSE probe using the Python standard library. It records semantic deltas and usage when the endpoint supplies it. It does not infer tokens from chunk counts, execute returned tools/code, generate large synthetic prompts or supply an open-loop arrival process. Use the pinned engine's native benchmark harness for controlled token workloads and detailed kernel/service measurements, and compare against this client view.

The probe accepts one completion per request (n=1) and requires a completed choice with a recognized finish reason. [DONE] alone cannot mark an empty or truncated stream successful. Tool-call identifiers and indices alone do not count as first semantic output; function names and argument text do. ok measures protocol completion, not answer quality or SLO compliance. A missing usage count stays unknown, and any failed request or missing count suppresses total output-token throughput. Both an origin URL and an API base ending in /v1 are accepted. The timeout applies to socket operations and is checked between bounded line reads, so it is not strict wall-clock cancellation.

python benchmarks/run_streaming.py --base-url http://127.0.0.1:8000 \
  --model Qwen/Qwen3.8-27B --requests-jsonl benchmarks/smoke.jsonl \
  --concurrency 1 --output benchmarks/results/local-smoke.json

The sample requests are integration smoke tests, not a model-quality benchmark. Before sharing results, attach node preflight JSON, engine/image manifests, model/drafter provenance, payload hashes and resolved launch arguments. No measured results are bundled with this atlas.

Back to contents ↑
GLOSSARY.md

Glossary: meaning, purpose and analogy

Use the analogies to build intuition, not as exact mathematics. Architecture-specific terms link back to the company reports; numeric and kernel claims are sourced in the evidence register, precision report and framework reviews. Unverified expansions are explicitly left as project/configuration names.

Token

Tokenizer unit. A piece of text or a model-specific input symbol, not necessarily a word.

Analogy: A page is cut into differently sized tiles; count tiles, not words.

Tokenizer / chat template

Text encoding and conversation serialization. Convert content and roles into the exact IDs/boundaries the model learned. Wrong tool/reasoning boundaries can break an otherwise fluent endpoint.

Analogy: The same message written in the wrong envelope format reaches the wrong desk.

Parameter / weight

Learned numerical value. Total weights drive storage; active weights approximate work per token only under stated accounting.

Analogy: All books on the shelves versus the books consulted for one question.

Dense

All ordinary feed-forward layers participate. No expert router chooses a small subset of the model’s MLPs per token.

Analogy: One permanent team reviews every paragraph.

MLP / FFN

Multilayer perceptron / feed-forward network. The transformations within a decoder block after or alongside attention; can be dense or replaced by experts.

Analogy: An editing workshop transforms what the reader has gathered.

MoE

Mixture of Experts. A router chooses a small number of expert networks per token. All experts still need storage somewhere unless deliberately offloaded.

Analogy: A large hospital employs many specialists; only a few see one patient.

Top-k routing

Select k routed experts. The number of selected experts is not the number resident in memory. Shared experts can add work beyond routed top-k.

Analogy: Two assigned specialists plus the general practitioner still count as three workers.

Shared expert

Always-used expert branch in the relevant architecture. Distinguish always-on shared computation from token-selected routed experts.

Analogy: A common reception desk supports every specialist appointment.

Latent MoE

Experts operating through a reduced representation. Kimi projects into a smaller expert space and back. Layer dimensions and kernels must match that interface.

Analogy: Specialists exchange compact forms rather than the entire case folder.

Attention

Context-dependent information lookup. Query/key comparisons determine how values contribute to a token’s representation.

Analogy: A reader searches previous notes before choosing the next sentence.

MHA / GQA / MQA

Multi-head / grouped-query / multi-query attention. Query heads can share key/value heads, reducing cached K/V. Always read explicit dimensions.

Analogy: Several readers share fewer reference notebooks.

KV cache

Key/value cache. Saved attention data avoids recalculating past token projections. It grows with context in ordinary full attention.

Analogy: Keep the reference notes instead of rereading the entire source every time.

MLA

Multi-head Latent Attention. A model-specific compressed latent representation changes projection and cache layout. Ordinary GQA memory arithmetic is not a full MLA estimate.

Analogy: Compress the reference folder before storing it, then reconstruct what is needed.

DSA

DeepSeek Sparse Attention. An indexed selection mechanism reduces which past representations receive expensive attention. Index and selected-value caches are distinct.

Analogy: A catalog points to relevant shelves rather than scanning every book.

QSA

Qwen sparse-attention path. Qwen’s particular sparse/indexed attention implementation must be read with its own compression and index shapes.

Analogy: A different library uses a different catalog; shelf numbers are not interchangeable.

SWA

Sliding-window attention. Restricts a layer’s direct attention to a recent window; some architectures interleave global layers.

Analogy: Keep recent pages on the desk, occasionally consult the complete archive.

NoPE

No positional encoding in the named path. A specific attention path omits ordinary positional encoding; not a claim the whole model has no ordering information.

Analogy: One index sorts by content, while other mechanisms preserve sequence.

RoPE

Rotary position embedding. Transforms components according to position. Partial rotary dimensions can differ from total head size.

Analogy: Position stamps help distinguish otherwise similar entries.

YaRN

A RoPE context-extension method. Its configured scale/original window affects position handling; a larger limit alone does not guarantee useful long-context accuracy.

Analogy: Stretch the map’s coordinate scale, then check that landmarks still line up.

KDA

Kimi Delta Attention. A gated recurrent/linear-attention mechanism with its own state and convolution path. It cannot be treated as ordinary token KV alone.

Analogy: Maintain and revise a compact notebook as each sentence arrives.

GDN

Gated DeltaNet. A gated delta-rule recurrent attention family used in Qwen hybrids; its state dimension and cache slots affect concurrency.

Analogy: The notebook has rules for what to replace, retain or forget.

SSM / recurrent state

State-space model / persistent running representation. Serving terminology may group recurrent caches under SSM/Mamba labels even for different linear-attention architectures.

Analogy: A running summary survives between steps; copying it also consumes space.

Convolution state

Short recent-input history. Hybrid blocks may require convolution buffers as well as matrix state. Disaggregation and rollback must preserve both.

Analogy: The running summary also carries a small stack of the latest pages.

mHC

Manifold-constrained Hyper-Connections. Multiple residual streams with constrained mixing; GLM Flash and DeepSeek expose related configuration. Do not infer Qwen gated residuals are identical.

Analogy: Several parallel discussion threads exchange information under stability rules.

Sinkhorn iterations

Iterative matrix normalization. An implementation mechanism for constrained mixing; iteration count is a real architecture/accuracy parameter, not a free speed knob.

Analogy: Repeatedly balance row and column totals until the routing ledger is consistent.

Residual connection

Bypass added or mixed with a transformation. Allows information to flow around a block; newer architectures can mix several residual streams.

Analogy: Keep the original draft available while applying an edit.

RMSNorm

Root-mean-square normalization. Rescales activations using RMS statistics. Fusion can reduce kernel/communication overhead without intentionally changing the mathematical contract.

Analogy: Adjust loudness without rewriting the speech.

GELU / SwiGLU / SiTU

Activation/operator names. GELU and gated Swish-family operations differ; Kimi’s SiTU coefficients and scale placement are part of its checkpoint contract. This atlas does not invent an unverified long-form expansion for SiTU.

Analogy: Different workshop machines need different settings, even when their input sizes match.

PLE / n-gram tables

Checkpoint labels for lexical/table components. Flash Next contains a large separately reported n-gram component. The long-form PLE expansion was not independently established here; retain the literal config label.

Analogy: A large phrase dictionary still occupies shelves even when only one entry is looked up.

MTP / NextN

Multi-token prediction / implementation naming. Auxiliary prediction structures can support speculative serving. A legacy config key does not prove the current engine should use its MTP algorithm.

Analogy: An attached assistant proposes a continuation, but the main writer approves it.

DSpark

Speculative-decoding project/method name. Match target features, checkpoint, proposal semantics and verification implementation. No invented acronym expansion.

Analogy: A fast assistant sketches a phrase before the lead editor checks it.

EAGLE

Speculative-decoding family / engine algorithm option. An engine’s EAGLE switch can route a model’s built-in MTP implementation; it does not necessarily mean an unrelated external draft model.

Analogy: The software menu names a workflow, not always the exact assistant employed.

Speculative acceptance

Verified draft tokens retained. Use observed acceptance under the actual workload and sampling; simulated acceptance is a synthetic experiment.

Analogy: Count edits the lead editor really accepted, not edits assumed accepted.

BF16 / FP16

Brain floating point 16 / 16-bit floating point. Two-byte formats with different exponent/significand allocations. Neither spelling establishes every tensor’s effective storage dtype.

Analogy: Two measuring tapes with the same number of markings but different range/detail trade-offs.

FP8 / E4M3 / E5M2

8-bit floating point / exponent-mantissa allocations. Smaller numeric formats rely on scaling and calibrated/dynamic ranges. FP8 in a filename is not a complete tensor-level specification.

Analogy: Write shorter numbers but retain the scale printed beside them.

NVFP4

NVIDIA four-bit floating-point representation. Combines FP4 values with block/global scaling. Scale precision, block size, packing and kernels matter.

Analogy: A compact ruler with a small local scale legend for each group.

MXFP4 / MXFP8

Microscaled four-/eight-bit floating point. Microscaling is a different contract from NVFP4. Kimi native MXFP4/MXFP8 is not the same export as NVIDIA NVFP4.

Analogy: Two compression formats can use four-bit symbols yet different dictionaries.

W4A4 / W4A8 / W4A16

Weight bits / activation bits. Weight and activation precision are independent. These labels do not describe KV-cache or recurrent-state precision.

Analogy: The archived recipe, active worksheet and notebook can each use a different paper size.

FP8_PB_WO

ModelOpt per-block FP8 weight-only label. A loader must handle exported block scales, shapes and mixed-module dispatch. Runtime activation processing may still be implementation-specific.

Analogy: The storage label is not a complete instruction manual for the calculation.

PTQ / QAT

Post-training quantization / quantization-aware training. PTQ transforms a trained checkpoint; QAT exposes training to quantization effects. Either still needs task-quality verification.

Analogy: Shrink a completed drawing versus practice drawing on the smaller grid.

Calibration

Estimate numeric ranges using representative samples. Calibration data/algorithm can affect quality. Do not compare exports as if all four-bit artifacts were equivalent.

Analogy: Set a camera’s exposure using scenes similar to those it will actually photograph.

ModelOpt / compressed-tensors

NVIDIA optimization tooling / serialized quantization framework. Exporter metadata directs loading and kernels. These are tool/format names, not universal interchangeable quantization flags.

Analogy: Two manufacturers package similar components with different wiring diagrams.

GPU / HBM

Graphics processing unit / high-bandwidth memory. The compute accelerator and its local high-bandwidth storage. Advertised capacity is not fully available for weights.

Analogy: A workshop plus its fast on-site warehouse.

SM / compute capability

Streaming multiprocessor / GPU architecture feature level. Kernels are compiled for compatible GPU targets. B300 is not equivalent to every device marketed as Blackwell.

Analogy: Related engines can still require different compatible parts.

SXM / SXM6-AC

Module/interface designation / user-provided OEM label. The precise AC-labeled server variant was not independently identified. Check the actual supplier inventory and board topology.

Analogy: A component label is not a complete vehicle specification.

NVLink / NVSwitch

NVIDIA GPU interconnect / switching fabric. Fast GPU communication can make sharding practical; it does not make eight GPUs one uniformly shared memory allocation.

Analogy: Fast roads join warehouses, but stock still lives at particular locations.

PCIe / NUMA

Peripheral Component Interconnect Express / non-uniform memory access. Host-device paths and CPU-memory locality affect input, communication and startup.

Analogy: Distance to the loading dock matters even with a fast workshop.

TP

Tensor parallelism. Split tensor work across ranks; introduces communication. Smaller dense models can benefit more from replicas.

Analogy: Several people work on sections of the same sentence and must synchronize.

DP

Data parallelism. Independent request/data work; attention-DP and full-model replicas are not necessarily the same MoE topology.

Analogy: Several reception desks handle separate customers.

EP

Expert parallelism. Distribute experts across existing ranks and route tokens between them. Active expert count does not multiply available memory.

Analogy: Specialists occupy different buildings and cases travel between them.

PP

Pipeline parallelism. Place sequential layer stages on different ranks. Scheduling bubbles and batch behavior affect utilization.

Analogy: An assembly line waits when the previous station has not delivered its part.

CP / DCP

Context parallelism / decode context parallelism. Split context-related attention work/cache under engine-specific semantics. Do not blindly divide all caches and state by this factor.

Analogy: Several archivists divide the reference pages, not necessarily every notebook.

Prefill / decode

Process prompt / generate subsequent tokens. Different workload phases with different compute/bandwidth profiles. Separating them adds transfer and scheduling costs.

Analogy: Read the brief first, then dictate the response a word at a time.

PD disaggregation

Prefill/decode separation. Dedicated pools can reduce interference, but hybrid state and KV transfer must match exactly.

Analogy: Transfer the case from a reading team to a writing team without losing notes.

Continuous batching

Dynamic request scheduling. Completed requests leave and new ones enter execution without waiting for a fixed batch to finish.

Analogy: A restaurant fills available seats as diners leave.

Paged attention / prefix cache

Managed KV blocks / reuse of shared prompt work. Paging improves allocation behavior; prefix caching reuses matching work. Neither proves hybrid-state or cross-tenant isolation is correct.

Analogy: Manage notebooks in reusable page blocks and keep copies of common introductions.

Radix cache

Prefix tree organized cache. Shared token prefixes can be represented by common tree paths. Eviction, state snapshots and tenant isolation still matter.

Analogy: Many routes share the same first streets before branching.

CUDA Graph

Recorded GPU execution graph. Replays work with less launch overhead under shape/state constraints; graph capture reserves memory and needs correctness testing.

Analogy: Record a repeatable production sequence instead of issuing every instruction again.

GEMM

General matrix multiplication. Core tensor operation. Fused or low-precision kernels are useful only for supported shapes, scales and hardware.

Analogy: A specialized press processes many entries together.

FlashInfer / FlashMLA / DeepGEMM

Kernel-library project names. Different libraries implement attention, latent-attention or matrix/MoE paths. Presence in an image does not prove the intended kernel was selected.

Analogy: A toolkit may contain the correct wrench, but the mechanic still has to use it.

TRT-LLM / CUTLASS

TensorRT-LLM / CUDA templates for linear-algebra subroutines. Kernel/backend families may be called by vLLM or SGLang; using a TRT-LLM MoE kernel does not replace the entire serving engine.

Analogy: A workshop can use another maker’s machine without changing ownership.

NCCL / DeepEP / all-reduce / all-to-all

NVIDIA collective communications / expert communication library / collective patterns. All-reduce combines values; all-to-all redistributes different payloads among ranks. Communication topology must match parallelism.

Analogy: A group totals everyone’s results, or sends different cases to the right specialists.

NIXL / RDMA

NVIDIA inference-transfer library (NIXL) / remote direct memory access. Transfer infrastructure used in some disaggregated designs. Exact buffer/state layouts remain an engine contract.

Analogy: A courier can move a folder quickly but cannot fix missing pages.

vLLM / SGLang

Serving-runtime project names. Both coordinate model execution, scheduling and kernels. Their argument names and support details differ.

Analogy: Two workshop operating systems, each with different compatible machinery.

KServe / Kubernetes

Serving control plane / cluster orchestration. KServe manages serving resources on Kubernetes; it is not a replacement numerical inference engine.

Analogy: The building manager assigns rooms and health checks; the workshop still performs the work.

CRD / CR / controller

Custom Resource Definition / Custom Resource / reconciliation program. The CRD defines an API shape; a CR is an instance; the controller acts on it. Schema acceptance alone does not prove model readiness.

Analogy: A form template, a completed order, and the staff who actually fulfill it.

InferenceService / LLMInferenceService

KServe resource kinds. The ordinary serving resource and LLM-specialized coordination surface have different version/integration requirements.

Analogy: A standard booking versus a coordinated multi-room production order.

LWS / EPP / Gateway API

LeaderWorkerSet / endpoint picker / Kubernetes gateway API. Building blocks for coordinated workers and routing; exact installation and engine adapters must be validated.

Analogy: Keep a team together and send each customer to the correct entrance.

PVC / image digest

PersistentVolumeClaim / immutable container content identifier. The PVC supplies storage; a digest pins image content. A syntactically valid digest does not prove availability or model compatibility.

Analogy: A warehouse reservation and a sealed versioned toolbox.

TTFT / TPOT / ITL

Time to first token / time per output token / inter-token latency. Different latency measurements. Semantic SSE chunks are not guaranteed to be individual tokens.

Analogy: Wait for the speaker to begin, then measure pace; sentence delivery is not syllable timing.

Throughput / goodput / SLO

Work per time / acceptable work per time / service-level objective. More tokens per second can hide poor tail latency or failures. Goodput counts work meeting declared requirements.

Analogy: Meals produced versus meals delivered correctly and on time.

p50 / p95 / p99

Latency percentiles. Describe distribution tails, not different forms of average. State how failed requests are handled.

Analogy: Most customers wait briefly, but the longest queues still matter.

SSE / API / HTTP

Server-sent events / application programming interface / Hypertext Transfer Protocol. Transport and request interfaces. Correct transport does not guarantee correct tokenization or tool semantics.

Analogy: The telephone line works, but the participants must still speak the same protocol.

GB / GiB

Decimal gigabyte / binary gibibyte. One GB is 1e9 bytes; one GiB is 2^30 bytes. Never subtract capacities expressed in different units without converting.

Analogy: Kilometers and miles can describe the same road but not with the same number.

SHA / pin / nightly

Content identifier / immutable selection / frequently rebuilt development artifact. Pin checkpoint and image independently. Main source, a release tag and a nightly image are different objects.

Analogy: A dated blueprint does not identify the exact assembled machine.

Back to contents ↑
LIMITATIONS.md

Verification boundaries and unresolved work

This is a research and engineering atlas. Exact checkpoint/image compatibility still requires runtime validation. It was assembled and audited on October 15, 2026 using publisher cards and configurations, engine recipes, selected source files and pull-request/release metadata. The audit record documents corrections and file coverage.

Completed

Eight model-family directories contain architecture dimensions, technical explanations, precision variants, serving hypotheses, validation plans and source links. The audit resolved exact publisher and derivative revisions, checked immutable metadata, revisited engine releases and main references, and rechecked selected implementation files and relevant PRs. Local code and document checks are reported in VALIDATION. HTTP success alone is not counted as verification of a claim; retrieval receipts and reviewer findings are recorded separately.

The subsequent engine experiment roadmap expands the stable/main/nightly research into proposed investigations. It includes a manual experiment-record template, not a new execution system. Its applicability checks and promotion thresholds must be completed for the actual image, node and workload. Existing profile source references remain their original reviewed snapshots; new research does not silently certify those profiles against a newer nightly.

Not completed or not available

No model weight tensors were downloaded and no inference engine was installed or run. Limited safetensors header metadata was read to check GLM parameter accounting. No B300 node was exercised, and no GPU correctness, throughput, latency, memory or power measurement was performed. No deployed KServe cluster was changed or validated. No nightly container was pulled or tested; source inspection does not certify a nightly binary.

The original assembly failed to clone vLLM and SGLang because that environment could not resolve GitHub; clone-attempt.log preserves that historical result. HTTPS retrieval and the atlas repository's Git fetch succeeded during this audit. Full upstream clones were not repeated. Selected immutable source files were retrieved directly, and clone_upstreams.py remains available for pinned checkouts. Complete upstream repositories are not bundled.

The exact OEM string “B300 SXM6-AC” was supplied by the user and was not independently identified. The hardware discussion uses NVIDIA’s B300 documentation conditionally; CPU, networking, physical topology, cooling, firmware, power limits and usable memory need actual node inventory. A DGX B300 reference specification does not identify the user's server.

Configuration fields are curated projections, not complete original configs. Exact model and variant revisions resolved in the audit are recorded in the model records, profiles and evidence lock. Hypothetical or unidentified variants retain null identities. Immutable links and response hashes improve traceability, but complete raw cards/configs and source trees are not bundled. Mutable recipes and branch heads remain dated observations. Local checksums establish consistency of local files, not historical authenticity or model quality.

Several precision requests have no independently audited exact release: in particular a fully FP8 Kimi K3 is not established. Some derivative repositories are supported by an engine recipe rather than a separately inspected complete export. These distinctions remain visible in the catalog and profiles; missing facts are not filled with neighboring model values.

DeepSeek's family parameter headlines are not an exact July-31 tensor census. GLM's approximately 743B target and 753B full checkpoint are distinguished using the extra MTP layer's header metadata; the accounting is not a GPU memory measurement. Advertised context limits do not establish accuracy or practical concurrency at that length. Licenses in the catalog are identification notes; the applicable base and derivative terms require separate inspection.

Meaning of “best” in this repository

Recommendations are starting hypotheses justified by memory architecture, available implementation paths and source evidence. A winner requires the same workload, input/output distribution, quality threshold, latency target, hardware, effective precision and natural speculative acceptance. Published author benchmarks are not reproduced results. In particular, simulated-acceptance Kimi DSpark results cannot certify application speedup.

VALIDATION.md

Local validation results

Audit and engine-expansion retrieval date: October 15, 2026 UTC (October 16 in Europe/Paris for the expansion). Supported interpreter used for the recorded local checks: Python 3.12.14.

Actual executions

68 unit tests passed on Python 3.12.14. The documented supported minimum remains Python 3.10. The suite covers memory arithmetic, immutable metadata identity, launch/probe gates, profile topology and context consistency, synthetic-acceptance rejection, redirect handling, exclusive checkout destinations, KServe rendering guards, streaming completion semantics, catalog integrity and offline HTML generation. Network behavior and engine invocations in the tests use mocks.

The repository validator passes for 8 model families, 6 companies and 34 profiles. Model JSON records and individual profile JSON files match their catalog copies. JSON parsing, Python compilation, source IDs and local Markdown links pass. Exact current file/link/source counts are in local-validation.json. The engine expansion adds documentation and a manual JSON run-record template; it adds no execution code or measured serving results.

Seven CLI smoke checks passed: weight arithmetic, ordinary GQA KV arithmetic, guarded dry-run launch, dry-run clone commands, metadata listing without network, benchmark help and KServe renderer help. The illustrative calculations returned 2.8e12 bytes for uniform FP8 at 2.8T parameters and 88 GiB for the stated Mistral KV example. These are calculations, not GPU allocation measurements.

The offline edition is rebuilt from the corrected Markdown. build_atlas.py --check checks freshness; the HTML tests check escaping, internal links, duplicate IDs, formatting and embedded source hashes. SHA256SUMS covers the committed artifacts except the checksum manifest itself.

Primary-source retrieval and review

During the original audit, all eight base model APIs and immutable configurations were independently retrieved by the coordinator, confirming revision, layer count and hidden size. Model reviewers checked the full curated projections and exact derivative metadata. The initial 62 source URLs and all 135 URLs in that audit's integrated register returned HTTP 200 in separate access checks. See the audit report for those independent reviews and corrections. The subsequent engine update records its additional primary-source research and reviews separately. Accessibility alone does not establish claim accuracy.

Actual Hub and GitHub HTTPS retrieval succeeded. During the engine expansion, GitHub REST requests hit an unauthenticated rate limit; official HTML, raw files and Git references supplied the cited alternatives. Temporary filtered/shallow upstream Git metadata was used where documented for release-versus-main source comparison. The original full-clone failures remain historical evidence; complete upstream clones were not repeated. The metadata downloader's mocked tests are distinct from an end-to-end integration test of that command against the Hub.

Explicitly not tested

No weight tensors were downloaded, no inference engine or serving container was installed or run, and no B300 model-quality, throughput, latency, power or GPU-memory measurement was performed. Only metadata, including limited GLM safetensors headers, was retrieved. No natural speculative acceptance was measured. No container image or nightly wheel was downloaded or tested. No Kubernetes admission check or deployment was performed. KServe unit tests use synthetic image digests to exercise syntax and structure without a registry lookup.

Reproduce

Use Python 3.10 or newer. The recorded audit used:

python3.12 scripts/validate_repo.py
python3.12 -m unittest discover -s tests -v
python3.12 scripts/build_atlas.py --check

Full unit output is in tests/last-run.txt. Passing local checks does not certify a serving configuration.

companies/alibaba-qwen/qwen3.8-27b/architecture.md

Qwen 3.8 27B: architecture

Data flow

Vision/text → [GDN → GDN → GDN → gated full attention] × 16, with dense MLPs → output head; optional MTP.

How the parts work together

Three Gated DeltaNet layers are followed by a gated full-attention layer, repeated through the text backbone. The linear-attention path maintains a recurrent matrix state with a short convolutional component. Full-attention layers retain ordinary token-history cache. Consequently, the model has both a context-dependent cache and a concurrency-dependent state pool.

The key and value head counts in the linear path differ. The full-attention path has its own query-head count, KV-head count and head dimension. Deriving head dimensions by dividing hidden width by a familiar head count would produce the wrong shapes here; use the explicit configuration.

The feed-forward network is dense, not MoE. Every token traverses those linears; there is no expert-parallel switch that turns it into a sparsely activated 27B model. The vision encoder is shared infrastructure for image/video inputs, with spatial merging and temporal patching before language processing.

MTP is an optional additional serving path. It changes scheduling and temporary-state needs, not the base model’s dense-versus-MoE identity. The official context extension beyond the native window is not evidence that every engine, quantization and workload preserves useful accuracy at that length.

The architecture facts above are drawn from the publisher card and configuration; the workload consequences are analytical interpretation. qwen3.8-27b-CARD qwen3.8-27b-CFG

Configuration extraction

This is a selected, normalized projection, not a byte-for-byte copy of the complete config. Field labels have been made comparable across models. Layer counts are derived from text_config.layer_types; rotary_dim is head_dim × partial_rotary_factor; nextn_layers corresponds to text_config.mtp_num_hidden_layers. The extended-context claim comes from the card rather than the config.

Source for the extraction: qwen3.8-27b-CFG. Headline counts, modality descriptions and advertised extended context are additionally drawn from qwen3.8-27b-CARD. A transformers_version metadata field is not by itself proof of a minimum compatible package version.

Memory and performance consequences

A TP1 process is a memory-planning candidate on a 288 GB GPU. Multiplying the publisher's rounded 27B language-model headline by two bytes gives approximately 54 GB (50.3 GiB) at BF16. This is not a checkpoint byte census or a strict lower bound; vision, auxiliary modules and runtime allocations need separate accounting. Compare independent replicas with sharded layouts for total node goodput at the same workload and latency target.

For the 16 full-attention layers, conventional unpadded BF16 KV payload is 2 × 16 × 4 × 256 × 262144 × 2 bytes, or 16 GiB per native-length sequence. The recurrent matrix alone is 48 × 48 × 128 × 128 × 4 bytes, or 144 MiB per state slot at FP32. Convolution state, cached snapshots, speculative verification and allocator overhead are additional. Neither calculation measures an engine's complete state pool.

The numeric examples are calculations from stated dimensions and assumptions, not measured allocations. Memory mathematics explains the formulas and their limits. qwen3.8-27b-CARD qwen3.8-27b-CFG HW

What this report does not establish

Actual TP1 NVFP4 kernel efficiency on this B300 firmware/container combination; optimal hybrid-cache split; useful quality at the extended context; performance under the user’s language and tool distributions.

The public artifacts do not disclose every training-data, optimization or implementation detail. This report does not fill missing details with assumptions or present a complete formal reconstruction of private training.

companies/alibaba-qwen/qwen3.8-27b/precision.md

Qwen 3.8 27B: precision and provenance

VariantExact artifactActual precision contractEvidence
bf16Qwen/Qwen3.8-27BBF16 declared by text configPublisher card and pinned config inspected
fp8Qwen/Qwen3.8-27B-FP8E4M3 FP8 with 128×128 weight blocks and excluded modulesPublisher card and pinned config inspected
nvfp4nvidia/Qwen3.8-27B-NVFP4NVFP4 MLP/head + FP8 attention projections; MTP excluded from quantization mapNVIDIA card and pinned quantization metadata inspected

Interpretation

Keep the BF16, official FP8 and NVIDIA mixed export as separate comparisons. The official FP8 config declares dynamic activations and 128×128 weight blocks; its exclusion list includes the vision tower, language head and selected recurrent components. The NVIDIA map assigns NVFP4 to 193 MLP/head module entries and FP8 to 208 attention projection entries, and excludes mtp*. These are metadata entries, not a tensor-byte census. qwen3.8-27b-FP8-CFG qwen3.8-27b-NV-CFG

Producer-version conflict: NVIDIA's card names ModelOpt 0.48.0 and local-Hessian calibration, but both pinned config.json and hf_quant_config.json record producer version 0.47.0.dev80+g913f5e224. The audit cannot reconcile the exporter version from these artifacts; preserve both observations when reproducing the checkpoint. qwen3.8-27b-NV-QUANT

The rounded weight arithmetic makes one B300 a plausible starting point; startup fit at the requested workload remains untested. NVFP4 may reduce bandwidth or permit more cache capacity, but quantization and kernel-launch overhead can offset that gain at low batch sizes. Benchmark the actual workload and retain BF16 as the quality reference.

qwen3.8-27b-CARD qwen3.8-27b-NV qwen3.8-27b-FP8 qwen3.8-27b-VR

Deployment rule

Pin the model revision, tokenizer/processor revision, quantization configuration, runtime commit and image digest together. A root BF16 dtype can coexist with quantized weights. The real contract includes serialized tensor types, scales, grouping, excluded modules, activation precision and runtime repacking. Do not cast a quantized checkpoint to BF16 and call it the original BF16 reference.

For every candidate, compare target-only quality against the appropriate reference before enabling lower-precision KV or speculation. Precision primer distinguishes NVFP4, MXFP4, FP8 and BF16. NV4 TE

License provenance

Apache-2.0 on base; also review derivative terms. This is a provenance flag, not legal clearance. The ZIP contains no model weights. Original model terms and derivative exporter terms remain separate from this repository’s original code and prose.

companies/alibaba-qwen/qwen3.8-27b/serving.md

Qwen 3.8 27B: serving on 8×B300

Status: unbenchmarked local candidates. Publisher and engine recipes document support; this audit did not run an engine or benchmark the node.

Engine and topology

Begin with vLLM TP1 BF16 at 32K context, then the same FP8 and NVFP4 workload. Repeat with SGLang TP1. Keep vision enabled for multimodal validation and explicitly constrain its allowed input sizes; a text-only optimization must be a separate profile.

The supplied TP1 settings are controlled local experiments. NVIDIA's card documents a TP4 vLLM example and a SGLang example for its exact export; the current SGLang cookbook also lists that export. The cookbook pins FP8 KV, whereas the supplied SGLang comparison profiles pin BF16 KV. Record this difference and resolve vLLM's auto cache dtype before comparing results. The vLLM recipe's low-latency NVFP4 example uses an Inferact artifact, so its measurements do not establish NVIDIA-export performance.

Compare per-GPU replicas with TP2 and TP4 only after measuring single-request latency. The NVIDIA card’s TP4 long-context example is not evidence that TP4 maximizes node throughput for ordinary chat. For hybrid prefix caching, verify the recurrent-state path, not just token KV hits.

Use the correct thinking mode and sampling settings in benchmarks. Qwen’s documented thinking and non-thinking modes do not have identical generation defaults, so matching only temperature can still leave an unfair comparison.

Base, official FP8 and NVIDIA metadata revisions are pinned in sources and model.json. Pin the tokenizer/processor and actual runtime image as well. A revision identifies source artifacts; it does not validate installed kernels, multimodal execution or MTP behavior.

qwen3.8-27b-VR qwen3.8-27b-GR qwen3.8-27b-CARD qwen3.8-27b-NV HW

Experiment sequence

Measure TP1/2/4 with 1/8/32/128 concurrent requests; BF16/FP8/mixed NVFP4; 4K/32K/128K contexts; text-only versus image inputs; MTP off versus supported depths. Keep request payloads and thinking effort frozen.

First capture actual GPU memory, compute capability, peer topology and software versions with python scripts/preflight.py. Then resolve immutable model metadata, inspect the rendered profile and run the target-only correctness suite. Profiles live in profiles.

Acceptance gates

A successful load is necessary but insufficient. Require valid streaming framing, finite outputs, correctly separated reasoning, valid tool calls where supported, repeatable quality tests, bounded memory at load, and correct cache/state behavior after cancellation. Only then compare latency and throughput.

For production, place one sharded model process group inside a pod that owns its whole GPU group. Do not make eight independent KServe replicas and expect them to form TP8. Start with colocated prefill and decode; adopt disaggregation only after it wins the workload comparison and state transfer is correct. KS KSLLM

Parameters are not magic constants

The profile’s context cap is an application policy. Tensor parallelism is a starting layout. Memory utilization reserves room for non-weight allocations; changing it can trade cache space against startup headroom. Batch-token and sequence caps trade prefill efficiency against memory and queueing. Speculative depth trades draft/verification cost against naturally accepted tokens. Each must be varied one at a time before interaction testing.

Remaining uncertainty

Actual TP1 NVFP4 kernel efficiency on this B300 firmware/container combination; optimal hybrid-cache split; useful quality at the extended context; performance under the user’s language and tool distributions.

companies/alibaba-qwen/qwen3.8-flash-next/architecture.md

Qwen 3.8 Flash Next: architecture

Data flow

Text/image/video inputs → vision processing where needed + token embeddings → 12 groups of three GDN/MoE layers and one QSA/MoE layer → output. Gated residual branches span the backbone; n-gram/PLE features enter at its second layer. MTP adds an optional serving path.

How the parts work together

The backbone combines three recurrent Gated DeltaNet layers with a sparse-attention layer in each four-layer group. Qwen describes a selection budget of 512 four-token micro-blocks, or 2,048 tokens. That budget limits selected attention history; it is not the model's context limit or a complete cache-size formula. The output remains autoregressive.

A large n-gram embedding table provides an additional lookup path for local token patterns. It changes the balance between arithmetic and table access. The PLE configuration identifies where that representation is injected; treating it as an ordinary vocabulary embedding or dropping it because it is not in the 125B headline would change the model.

The main MoE selects ten routed experts plus one shared expert. Alongside it, the publisher describes four residual branches with data-dependent read gates and per-branch write gates. The configuration field hc_count alone does not establish equivalence to another model family's hyper-connection mechanism.

When MTP is enabled, its loader must respect its own expert precision, which differs from the main routed experts in NVIDIA's export. A target-only load cannot establish that this speculative path works.

The architecture facts above are drawn from the publisher card and configuration; the workload consequences are analytical interpretation. qwen3.8-flash-next-CARD qwen3.8-flash-next-CFG

Configuration extraction

This is a selected, normalized projection, not a byte-for-byte copy of the complete config. Counts are derived from text_config.layer_types; its twelve full_attention labels implement QSA according to the publisher card. rotary_dim is head_dim × partial_rotary_factor, and the two linear head dimensions both equal 128. The shared-expert count and extended-context claim come from the card. ple_layer_ids: [2] denotes the second backbone layer; the NVIDIA quantization map names its zero-based tensor path layers.1.ple. qwen3.8-flash-next-NV-CFG

Memory and performance consequences

The publisher's rounded language-model accounting is 125B main + 51B n-gram + 4B MTP, approximately 180B in that scope. This sum is not an exact tensor census and does not separately account for the vision encoder. The 6B activated headline applies to the main path; it should not be divided by this combined scope to claim a precisely defined sparsity ratio.

NVIDIA's quantization metadata marks the n-gram embedding as FP8. Applying one byte to the rounded 51B headline gives approximately 51 GB (47.5 GiB), before scales, padding and placement effects. The main experts' four-bit arithmetic does not cover this component. Measure table placement, lookup locality, transfer bandwidth and tail latency when comparing host offload with GPU residency. qwen3.8-flash-next-NV-QUANT

The numeric examples are calculations from stated dimensions and assumptions, not measured allocations. Memory mathematics explains the formulas and their limits. qwen3.8-flash-next-CARD qwen3.8-flash-next-CFG HW

What this report does not establish

B300 load, quality and performance for the documented SGLang NVIDIA TP1 recipe; n-gram placement costs; full checkpoint tensor-byte census; production throughput under realistic prefix reuse.

The public artifacts do not disclose every training-data, optimization or implementation detail. This report does not fill missing details with assumptions or present a complete formal reconstruction of private training.

Qwen 3.8 Flash Next: precision and provenance

VariantExact artifactActual precision contractEvidence
bf16Qwen/Qwen3.8-Flash-NextBF16 declared by text configPublisher card and pinned config inspected
fp8Qwen/Qwen3.8-Flash-Next-FP8FP8 routed experts and PLE embedding; attention/shared experts excludedPublisher card and pinned config inspected
nvfp4nvidia/Qwen3.8-Flash-Next-NVFP4Main routed experts NVFP4; MTP experts and PLE FP8; attention/shared BF16 per publisherNVIDIA card and pinned quantization metadata inspected

Interpretation

The official FP8 config declares dynamic activations and 128×128 weight blocks, excludes attention/shared-expert modules, and explicitly includes the PLE n-gram embedding. The card and config were directly inspected at revision 236dfdf285828023ca3bcd3f37366c58a3469b13. qwen3.8-flash-next-FP8-CFG

NVIDIA describes W4A4 NVFP4 for the main routed experts, BF16 attention/shared components, 128×128 block FP8 for MTP routed experts, and per-tensor FP8 for the PLE embedding. The pinned maps confirm 48 NVFP4 expert-module entries, one FP8 PLE entry and one block-FP8 MTP entry. They use different MTP algorithm labels: FP8_PB_WO in config.json and FP8_BLOCK_SCALES in hf_quant_config.json. Record both files rather than replacing them with a single inferred format label. qwen3.8-flash-next-NV-CFG qwen3.8-flash-next-NV-QUANT

The card names vLLM commit d4d703caf908786416585ceb1f369e2e0363358b for target-only support and PR #55513 for MTP. GitHub confirms that PR merged on September 8, 2026 as 60ad959b6f1a5c8f602edbd608c8decbc0788c50. The installed image must contain the relevant changes. NVIDIA's TP8 MTP example enables expert parallelism: the 640-wide expert intermediate dimension divided by eight would be 80, which does not satisfy a 128-wide block boundary. This explains the documented constraint; it is not a local load test.

qwen3.8-flash-next-CARD qwen3.8-flash-next-NV qwen3.8-flash-next-VR QPR

Deployment rule

Pin the model revision, tokenizer/processor revision, quantization configuration, runtime commit and image digest together. A root BF16 dtype can coexist with quantized weights. The real contract includes serialized tensor types, scales, grouping, excluded modules, activation precision and runtime repacking. Do not cast a quantized checkpoint to BF16 and call it the original BF16 reference.

For every candidate, compare target-only quality against the appropriate reference before enabling lower-precision KV or speculation. Precision primer distinguishes NVFP4, MXFP4, FP8 and BF16. NV4 TE

License provenance

Qwen Community License 1.0; NVIDIA derivative also names NVIDIA Open Model License. This is a provenance flag, not legal clearance. The ZIP contains no model weights. Original model terms and derivative exporter terms remain separate from this repository’s original code and prose.

companies/alibaba-qwen/qwen3.8-flash-next/serving.md

Qwen 3.8 Flash Next: serving on 8×B300

Status: unbenchmarked local candidates. Publisher and engine recipes document support; this audit did not run an engine or benchmark the node.

Engine and topology

The supplied vLLM target-only candidate uses the exact NVIDIA export at TP8 and 65,536 tokens. The separate TP8 MTP candidate adds expert parallelism and one speculative token, following NVIDIA's layout while using a lower context cap. The supplied SGLang profile adapts its documented B300 TP1 target-only cell, with local caps of 65,536 tokens, 32 requests and a 0.82 static-memory fraction.

SGLang PR #41046 added exact NVIDIA-export recipes for B200/B300/GB300 and names version 0.5.20 or later. Its immutable generator selects TP1, FlashInfer linear-attention prefill/decode and BF16 recurrent state; it explicitly marks these cells verified: false. This supplies a concrete compatibility candidate, not B300 measurements. The profile retains that boundary. qwen3.8-flash-next-GPR qwen3.8-flash-next-GR-SOURCE

The base, official FP8 and NVIDIA metadata revisions are recorded in sources and model.json. NVIDIA's card supplies the exact NVFP4 TP8/MTP example; the current vLLM recipe's principal TP4+EP example uses the official FP8 export. Keep their checkpoint identities, KV settings and speculative depths distinct. Pin the actual runtime image before execution.

qwen3.8-flash-next-VR qwen3.8-flash-next-CARD qwen3.8-flash-next-NV HW

Experiment sequence

Compare SGLang TP1 target-only with vLLM TP8 target-only, accounting for total GPUs consumed and effective cache/state dtypes. Then evaluate TP4 capacity experiments, TP8+EP with one-token MTP, and alternative proposal depths after accuracy checks. Track n-gram placement, lookup stalls, recurrent-state allocation, indexer time and total node goodput.

Use the pinned publisher card and tokenizer template for thinking controls and sampling. The current SGLang cookbook's claim that this model always thinks conflicts with the publisher card, which documents enable_thinking and separate thinking/non-thinking sampling settings. Treat that cookbook prose as stale. qwen3.8-flash-next-GR

First capture actual GPU memory, compute capability, peer topology and software versions with python scripts/preflight.py. Then resolve immutable model metadata, inspect the rendered profile and run the target-only correctness suite. Profiles live in profiles.

Acceptance gates

A successful load is necessary but insufficient. Require valid streaming framing, finite outputs, correctly separated reasoning, valid tool calls where supported, repeatable quality tests, bounded memory at load, and correct cache/state behavior after cancellation. Only then compare latency and throughput.

For production, place one sharded model process group inside a pod that owns its whole GPU group. Do not make eight independent KServe replicas and expect them to form TP8. Start with colocated prefill and decode; adopt disaggregation only after it wins the workload comparison and state transfer is correct. KS KSLLM

Parameters are not magic constants

The profile’s context cap is an application policy. Tensor parallelism is a starting layout. Memory utilization reserves room for non-weight allocations; changing it can trade cache space against startup headroom. Batch-token and sequence caps trade prefill efficiency against memory and queueing. Speculative depth trades draft/verification cost against naturally accepted tokens. Each must be varied one at a time before interaction testing.

Remaining uncertainty

B300 load, quality and performance for the documented SGLang NVIDIA TP1 recipe; n-gram placement costs; full checkpoint tensor-byte census; production throughput under realistic prefix reuse.

companies/deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash-0731/architecture.md

DeepSeek V4 Flash 0731: architecture

Data flow

Tokens → recent-window + compressed selected history → mHC streams → hash/routed experts → output; bundled DSpark features → proposals → target verifies.

How the parts work together

The configuration exposes several interacting mechanisms: compressed attention representations, indexed sparse retrieval, a short sliding window, multiple hyper-connected streams, and routed experts. Its compression schedule alternates different compression ratios after the initial uncompressed layers. Draft-related entries also appear in the serialized schedule, so its length must not be used as the main layer count.

The local window protects access to recent tokens while the compressed and selected representations provide longer-range access. A sparse index budget is a selection budget, not the model’s maximum context length. The maximum positional setting and the available GPU cache are again separate constraints.

The model uses a specific router score function and scaling, plus bounded SwiGLU behavior. A generic MoE wrapper that changes the gate normalization or clipping would alter inference even if every checkpoint tensor loaded successfully.

The release bundles DSpark-related structure, including selected target-layer features and a Markov component. The configured block size, an engine’s proposal count, and the number of tokens emitted after verification are different quantities. Never “correct” the checkpoint’s block setting to match an example CLI argument without checking that implementation’s semantics.

The architecture facts above are drawn from the publisher card and configuration; the workload consequences are analytical interpretation. deepseek-v4-flash-0731-CARD deepseek-v4-flash-0731-CFG

Configuration extraction

This is a selected, normalized projection, not a byte-for-byte copy of the complete config. Field labels have been made comparable across models. Layer indices explicitly state zero-based or one-based conventions.

That namespace does not make the checkpoint compatible with an ordinary MTP speculative method. The reference loop also confirms that target feature IDs 40, 41 and 42 are zero-based. Reference inference config, reference implementation.

Memory and performance consequences

Do not read quant_method=fp8 as an all-FP8 model: the same configuration explicitly sets expert_dtype=fp4. Likewise, a single generic GQA formula does not represent all compressed caches, indexes, windows and draft state in this architecture.

The vLLM recipe uses the preview-family headline 284B total / 13B active, while the SGLang cookbook labels the exact 0731 variant 304B / 13B. These differently scoped figures are not a verified main-plus-draft tensor census. The catalog keeps exact total and active counts unset until that accounting is reconciled; Hugging Face serialized-element totals also include packing and auxiliary tensors. vLLM recipe, SGLang cookbook.

A 1,048,576-token positional ceiling follows the configured YaRN factor 16 and original 65,536 positions; that equality does not measure usable cache or long-context quality. Memory mathematics explains the formulas and their limits. deepseek-v4-flash-0731-CARD deepseek-v4-flash-0731-CFG HW

What this report does not establish

Exact main-plus-draft parameter accounting; selected image and kernel provenance; local B300 capacity, throughput and application correctness; qualification of the local eight-GPU adaptations.

The public artifacts do not disclose every training-data, optimization or implementation detail. This report does not fill missing details with assumptions or present a complete formal reconstruction of private training.

companies/deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash-0731/precision.md

DeepSeek V4 Flash 0731: precision and provenance

Reverified 2026-10-15 (UTC) at 7872f01b1d1fe23eabc4c98b48bffcef5a386062. No checkpoint was loaded locally.

VariantExact artifactPrecision evidence
nativedeepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731Root config sets expert_dtype=fp4 and separately declares FP8 e4m3 with ue8m0 scales and 128×128 weight blocks

Interpretation

The official artifact is mixed precision. Its reference loader packs two FP4 expert values per byte and uses one E8M0 scale per 32 expert values. The 128×128 blocks in the root quantization config describe the FP8 path, not those FP4 expert groups. Other tensors can retain higher precision. This is not an all-FP8 checkpoint, and four-bit expert storage alone does not identify an NVFP4 export. Root config, reference implementation.

The vLLM recipe's optional FP4 indexer cache changes a separate attention-cache representation. Keep it off during the initial correctness comparison, then measure retrieval quality and performance when enabling it. The checkpoint's FP4 expert weights do not mandate that cache setting. vLLM recipe.

Engine execution precision can differ from serialized precision. Select the intended expert backend and inspect its actual load/repacking path before comparing memory or speed. SGLang documents flashinfer_mxfp4 for the exact 0731 FP4 route; the atlas's vLLM candidate follows the documented deep_gemm_mega_moe route. These names are not evidence that both execute the same weight and activation arithmetic. Publisher serving examples.

Deployment rule

Pin the model and tokenizer revisions, quantization configuration, runtime commit and image digest together. Root BF16 metadata can coexist with quantized weights. Inspect serialized tensors, scales, excluded modules, activation precision and runtime repacking. Casting a quantized checkpoint to BF16 does not reconstruct an original BF16 reference.

Compare target-only quality before changing cache precision or enabling DSpark. The precision primer explains the formats, and the serving report keeps publisher recipes separate from local experiments.

License provenance

The pinned LICENSE file is MIT. The same revision's model card explicitly applies that license to the repository and model weights. This verifies the named artifact's stated terms; it does not establish terms for separately named derivatives. No model weights are bundled here. LICENSE, model card.

companies/deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash-0731/serving.md

DeepSeek V4 Flash 0731: serving on 8×B300

Status: unbenchmarked local recommendations. The upstream recipes are evidence of documented support, not a benchmark of your node.

Engine and topology

Use the documented vLLM TP1/DP4 plus expert-parallel layout as the initial four-GPU baseline. The DP8/EP profile is an explicit eight-GPU adaptation. Expert parallelism reuses the data-parallel group; it is not another factor multiplying the GPU count. Both local profiles impose a 64K cap, FP8 KV and the DeepGEMM MegaMoE backend. The chosen image still needs to pass correctness checks for this exact revision. vLLM recipe.

The publisher also documents the exact 0731 checkpoint with SGLang TP4, flashinfer_mxfp4 and bundled DSpark. SGLang's cookbook reports an end-to-end run of that command on 4×GB300 with v0.5.16. This is upstream evidence, not a local eight-B300 result. The included SGLang TP8 target-only profile remains a compatibility probe because it changes that topology and omits speculation. Publisher card, SGLang cookbook.

Preserve the producer's encoding contract: this release supplies Python message-encoding/parsing helpers instead of a Jinja chat template. Check reasoning fields, tool boundaries and streaming. The engine parser names differ: vLLM documents deepseek_v4; SGLang documents reasoning parser deepseek-v4 and tool parser deepseekv4. A fluent answer alone does not verify that protocol. Encoding documentation, vLLM recipe, SGLang cookbook.

DSpark weights are bundled with the target. SGLang instructs users to omit a separate draft-model path; its documented default proposes five tokens and verifies six. The vLLM recipe uses seven proposals. These engine choices do not rewrite the checkpoint's dspark_block_size=5. Match sampling too: the publisher's vLLM example uses greedy drafting, while the current recipe selects probabilistic drafting. Treat each as a distinct experiment and keep the client sampling fixed. Publisher card, vLLM recipe, SGLang cookbook.

The 64K local cap is deliberately restrictive. DeepSeek recommends allowing up to 384K output tokens for high/max effort; those requests cannot fit this cap. Compare engines under the same shorter application budget, or create a separately qualified long-output profile. Publisher usage guidance.

Experiment sequence

Compare target-only with native DSpark, natural acceptance, and identical prompts. Test 4-GPU versus 8-GPU EP layouts, index-cache precision and 16K/64K/128K prompts. Validate tool calls, reasoning boundaries, EOS handling and the first token after a cancelled speculative request.

First capture actual GPU memory, compute capability, peer topology and software versions with python scripts/preflight.py. Then verify local metadata against the immutable revision in model.json, inspect the rendered profile and run the target-only correctness suite. Profiles live in profiles.

Acceptance gates

A successful load is necessary but insufficient. Require valid streaming framing, finite outputs, correctly separated reasoning, valid tool calls where supported, repeatable quality tests, bounded memory at load, and correct cache/state behavior after cancellation. Only then compare latency and throughput.

For production, place one sharded model process group inside a pod that owns its whole GPU group. Independent KServe replicas do not assemble a distributed TP/DP/EP engine group. Start with colocated prefill and decode; adopt disaggregation only after it wins the workload comparison and state transfer is correct. KS KSLLM

Parameters are not magic constants

The profile’s context cap is an application policy. Tensor parallelism is a starting layout. Memory utilization reserves room for non-weight allocations; changing it can trade cache space against startup headroom. Batch-token and sequence caps trade prefill efficiency against memory and queueing. Speculative depth trades draft/verification cost against naturally accepted tokens. Each must be varied one at a time before interaction testing.

Remaining uncertainty

Exact main-plus-draft parameter accounting; selected image and kernel provenance; local B300 capacity, throughput and application correctness; qualification of the local eight-GPU adaptations.

companies/google/gemma-4-31b-it/architecture.md

Gemma 4 31B IT: architecture

Data flow

Vision/text → [five local sliding layers → one global layer] ×10 with dense MLPs → tied output head + logit soft-cap → sampling.

How the parts work together

Five local sliding-attention layers are followed by one global layer. Local attention keeps recent context within a bounded window, while global layers provide long-distance interaction. This substantially changes the cache shape relative to 60 uniform full-attention layers.

Local and global attention have different KV-head counts and head dimensions. The publisher describes unified keys and values in the global layers. This same-layer behavior is distinct from the configuration’s num_kv_shared_layers: 0. A formula that allocates separate K and V buffers in every layer is therefore an assumption about storage, not a deduction from the config. Inspect the selected engine’s allocator before claiming an exact physical cache size.

The feed-forward blocks are dense and use the configured GELU approximation. Tied embeddings and output-head behavior matter both for loading and for quantization exclusions. Final logit soft-capping changes how scores reach the sampler; it must remain in the generation path.

Image features are processed through the vision tower before text generation. The presence of audio token identifiers elsewhere in a shared family configuration is not proof of an audio encoder in this specific variant. Capability must be checked at the concrete model, processor and runtime level.

The architecture facts above are drawn from the publisher card and configuration; the workload consequences are analytical interpretation. gemma-4-31b-it-CARD gemma-4-31b-it-CFG

Configuration extraction

This is a selected, normalized projection, not a byte-for-byte copy of the complete config. Field labels have been made comparable across models. Layer indices explicitly state zero-based or one-based conventions.

PropertyValue

model_typegemma4

Memory and performance consequences

The pinned base repository lists 62,546,338,248 bytes of BF16 safetensors files, including file headers (about 62.55 decimal GB). This weight magnitude makes TP1 a reasonable B300 candidate; actual startup headroom still needs measuring. The rounded 31B family label is not an exact tensor census. gemma-4-31b-it-BASE-TREE

Lower precision can free memory for more requests or longer inputs. Multiple TP1 replicas use separate GPUs and separate weight copies; they do not create extra GPUs. Compare their aggregate throughput and per-request latency with tensor sharding under the same node budget.

Local and global cache pools need compatible budgeting. A user who sends many maximum-length requests can exhaust global cache even though most layers use a short window. Vision preprocessing adds its own transient memory; count image size and number, not merely the number of HTTP requests.

Repository file metadata and any cache calculations are not measured GPU allocations. Memory mathematics explains the formulas and their limits. gemma-4-31b-it-CARD gemma-4-31b-it-CFG HW

What this report does not establish

Physical KV allocation under attention_k_eq_v in the selected engine; exact export-level SGLang compatibility; correct tool template at the final image revision; local B300 quality and latency.

The public artifacts do not disclose every training-data, optimization or implementation detail. This report does not fill missing details with assumptions or present a complete formal reconstruction of private training.

Gemma 4 31B IT: precision and provenance

VariantExact artifactActual precision contractEvidence
bf16google/gemma-4-31B-itBF16Pinned publisher card/config inspected
fp8RedHatAI/gemma-4-31B-it-FP8-blockW8A8, 128×128 weight blocks, dynamic activation groups of 128Pinned exporter card/config inspected
nvfp4RedHatAI/gemma-4-31B-it-NVFP4Text Linear W4A4 group 16, local activation scaling; vision/embedding/head retain precisionPinned exporter card/config inspected

Interpretation

Red Hat’s FP8-block configuration declares compressed-tensors, 128×128 weight blocks and dynamic activation groups of 128. The NVFP4 configuration declares nvfp4-pack-quantized, group size 16 and local activation scaling. The published recipes preserve vision, embeddings and the output head at their original precision. These descriptions identify the export contract; no weight tensors were loaded in this audit. gemma-4-31b-it-FP8-CFG gemma-4-31b-it-NV-CFG

The pinned tree metadata lists the following safetensors file totals, including headers. These are reported artifact sizes, not downloaded-byte measurements or GPU memory readings.

ArtifactListed weight-file bytesDecimal GBSource
Google BF1662,546,338,24862.546BASE-TREE
Red Hat FP8-block33,263,238,92833.263FP8-TREE
Red Hat NVFP423,265,401,14423.265NV-TREE

Dividing the listed BF16 file total by the NVFP4 total gives about 2.69×, so the exporter’s fourfold parameter-bit reduction does not describe the complete artifact size. Scales and higher precision components remain, and runtime caches and workspaces add further memory. Keep the pinned BF16 checkpoint as the reference for text, vision and tool tests.

gemma-4-31b-it-CARD gemma-4-31b-it-NV gemma-4-31b-it-FP8

Deployment rule

Pin the model revision, tokenizer/processor revision, quantization configuration, runtime commit and image digest together. A root BF16 dtype can coexist with quantized weights. The real contract includes serialized tensor types, scales, grouping, excluded modules, activation precision and runtime repacking. Do not cast a quantized checkpoint to BF16 and call it the original BF16 reference.

For every candidate, compare target-only quality against the appropriate reference before enabling lower-precision KV or speculation. Precision primer distinguishes NVFP4, MXFP4, FP8 and BF16. NV4 TE

License provenance

The pinned Google base card and both Red Hat cards identify Apache-2.0 and link to Google’s Gemma Apache 2.0 license page, rechecked on 2026-10-15. This records the published license identity. The ZIP contains no model weights. Original model terms and derivative exporter terms remain separate from this repository’s original code and prose.

companies/mistral-ai/mistral-medium-3.5/architecture.md

Mistral Medium 3.5: architecture

Data flow

Text / Pixtral vision → projector → 88 dense GQA blocks with YaRN → output head → Mistral encoding/tool/reasoning protocol.

How the parts work together

The language network uses dense feed-forward blocks and grouped-query attention. Many query heads share a smaller set of key/value heads. Unlike the hybrid models in this catalog, its text configuration does not declare a sliding window; full token history therefore drives attention-cache growth across the language layers.

The positional configuration extends a 4,096-position reference using YaRN with factor 64. The publisher identifies a concrete correction in commit c4be198050fb5789774a55b92ed697becfbf20ae: mscale_all_dim changed from 1.0 to 0.0. The pinned base config contains 0.0. The publisher warns that derivatives built with the earlier config can inherit degraded long-context behavior. mistral-medium-3.5-FIXCFG

The Pixtral vision encoder and multimodal projector process image inputs. Their precision exclusions are part of the official FP8 export, so the whole checkpoint is not homogeneous FP8. The text architecture class, vision architecture and tokenizer/encoding stack together define a valid model load.

The official repository contains both consolidated-format and Transformers-style weight shards. These are alternate representations, not six disjoint pieces of one larger model. Downloading both layouts approximately doubles weight download/storage volume. Startup transfer depends on which layout the loader reads; retaining both on disk does not imply loading both into GPU memory.

The architecture facts above are drawn from the publisher card and configuration; the workload consequences are analytical interpretation. mistral-medium-3.5-CARD mistral-medium-3.5-CFG

This is a selected, normalized projection, not a byte-for-byte copy of the complete config. Field labels have been made comparable across models. Layer indices explicitly state zero-based or one-based conventions.

Memory and performance consequences

At the pinned revision, each of the Transformers and consolidated indexes reports metadata.total_size = 133605834656 bytes: about 133.606 decimal GB or 124.430 GiB of tensor payload per alternative layout. The sum across both alternatives is 267.212 GB and must not be used as the weight size of one loaded model. Index payload sizes exclude safetensors headers, auxiliary files and runtime allocations. mistral-medium-3.5-HF-INDEX mistral-medium-3.5-NATIVE-INDEX

For one 262,144-token sequence, separate BF16 keys and values give 2 × 88 layers × 8 KV heads × 128 elements × 2 bytes × 262144 tokens = 94,489,280,512 bytes = 88 GiB across the model. At 32,768 tokens the same ideal element payload is 11 GiB. These calculations exclude padding, any head replication, cache scales and engine overhead. FP8 KV would halve the element payload if supported and validated; quantized weights do not automatically imply FP8 KV.

A single B300 can plausibly accommodate the FP8 weights and a constrained short-context workload, but this is not a long-context/concurrency guarantee. Establish correctness with the publisher’s TP8 example, then compare TP2, TP4 and a constrained TP1 probe under the same workload and total GPU budget.

The numeric examples are calculations from stated dimensions and assumptions, not measured allocations. Memory mathematics explains the formulas and their limits. mistral-medium-3.5-CARD mistral-medium-3.5-CFG HW

What this report does not establish

Installed-image and B300 compatibility at the pinned corrected revision; runtime memory beyond the reported weight payload; maximum safe concurrency and best TP width; no audited NVFP4 variant. SGLang support is documented upstream, but has not been run locally.

The public artifacts do not disclose every training-data, optimization or implementation detail. This report does not fill missing details with assumptions or present a complete formal reconstruction of private training.

Mistral Medium 3.5: precision and provenance

VariantExact artifactActual precision contractEvidence

Interpretation

The default export uses static-activation FP8 without the 128×128 weight-block scheme used by several other models in the list. Vision, projector and language-head exclusions remain higher precision. Do not force a block-FP8 loader or an unrelated NVFP4 mode onto these files.

This atlas does not invent a Mistral NVFP4 checkpoint: the requested Mistral variant was not specified that way, and no exact NVFP4 derivative was audited. A future conversion must begin from the corrected configuration and validate long-context and multimodal behavior.

mistral-medium-3.5-CARD

Deployment rule

Pin the model revision, tokenizer/processor revision, quantization configuration, runtime commit and image digest together. A root BF16 dtype can coexist with quantized weights. The real contract includes serialized tensor types, scales, grouping, excluded modules, activation precision and runtime repacking. Do not cast a quantized checkpoint to BF16 and call it the original BF16 reference.

For every candidate, compare target-only quality against the appropriate reference before enabling lower-precision KV or speculation. Precision primer distinguishes NVFP4, MXFP4, FP8 and BF16. NV4 TE

License provenance

The pinned LICENSE is titled Modified MIT License. Its revenue clause restricts exercise of the license rights when the company or employer’s global consolidated monthly revenue exceeded US$20 million in the preceding month, and directs affected users to Mistral for commercial licensing. The other card metadata does not describe this condition by itself. The ZIP contains no model weights. Original model terms and derivative exporter terms remain separate from this repository’s original code and prose.
