These are choices, not eight measured support claims. The profile index retains immutable identities and statuses. Preserve each model's encoding, modality, reasoning and tool constraints. DeepSeek's documented four-GPU run includes DSpark; removing speculation is a new target-only qualification step. GEXP-DEEPSEEK.

G01 — Stable, dated nightly and pinned-main qualification

Question and treatments. Compare v0.5.21, one dated nightly with resolved provenance, and a build from 4e0609ea277d09393d752fcb79181e3416d94c56 when the patch under study postdates that nightly. Keep the checkpoint and effective settings fixed. If dependencies or defaults necessarily change, report a whole-build comparison; isolate individual patches only through a separate controlled build pair.

Measurements and gate. Record load/capture time, model class, module quantization methods, attention/MoE/cache backends, effective weight/KV/state dtypes and startup pool capacities. Pass target-only quality, streaming, stop/EOS, reasoning, tools and supported image checks before load testing. Reject unexplained fallback, missing scales, NaNs, invalid protocol, OOM or excess quality regression. Loading failure is a result; do not substitute a neighboring derivative. GEXP-SRC-REG.

G02 — Recurrent-state and token-KV allocation

Question and treatments. Find the limiting pool for Qwen 27B, GLM Flash and Kimi. Begin without speculation. At fixed total memory reservation, compare the baseline split with lower/higher state allocations chosen from boot measurements. Use the build's documented --mamba-full-memory-ratio or --max-mamba-cache-size, recording which determines allocation. Fix active-state dtype, radix strategy, tracking interval and scheduler. Repeat feasible splits with speculation and ReplaySSM separately.

Measurements and gate. Retain token capacity, active/cached state slots, verify/replay allocation, effective request cap, retractions and component hit lengths. Do not transfer Qwen's slot formula to GLM or Flash Next's additional state. Exercise branching, partial hits, eviction, cancellation and speculative rejection. Reject state corruption, growing unreclaimed allocations or an unnoticed concurrency-cap change. Extra state is beneficial only if goodput improves without starving KV. GEXP-QWEN, GEXP-GLMFLASH, GEXP-PR40517.

G03 — Prefix reuse, core selection and HiCache pressure

Question and treatments. Compare GPU-only reuse, L1+L2, then L1+L2+L3 after backend provisioning. Use a no-reuse control and branching conversations whose prefixes cross measured device/host budgets. Within a compatible configuration, compare SGLANG_UNIFIED_RADIX_TREE_CORE_BACKEND=python and rust; the logged concrete core defines the treatment. Keep routing fixed so replicas receive equal reuse opportunities.

Measurements and gate. Separate cold, GPU-hit, host-hit and storage-hit runs. Record component hit lengths, recomputed tokens, eviction/retraction, CPU cost, transfer bytes/latency, host peak memory and storage I/O. Exercise splits, flushes, storage timeout and transfer cancellation. Reject contamination, incomplete hybrid-state restoration or leaks. If both selections resolve to Python, mark the core comparison inapplicable. Cross-instance reuse requires configured shared L3; L2 is instance-private. GEXP-SRC-TREECORE, GEXP-HICACHE.

G04 — Int8 cached-state checkpoints

Question and treatments. Compare --enable-int8-mamba-checkpoint off/on on a compatible hybrid model. First fix cached-slot count to measure memory/conversion cost; then fix total cache memory to measure useful retained-prefix capacity. Keep active-state and KV dtypes unchanged. This is present in stable source and does not require the latest nightly.

Measurements and gate. Include scale/conv memory, restoration cost, TTFT and hit rate. Evaluate long continuations after one restore and repeated branch/store/restore cycles, including arithmetic, tools and distant recall. Compare with uncached and full-precision cached controls. Reject quality loss or pressure merely displaced into the active pool. The temporal store is lossy; approximate component-capacity claims do not predict total process memory or application quality. GEXP-STABLE-MAMBA, GEXP-SRC-MAMBA.

G05 — Export precision and kernel dispatch

Question and treatments. Compare Qwen 27B's three exact exports at fixed KV/state precision. Separately qualify mixed NVIDIA Kimi/Flash Next and the exact Gemma derivative. For a fixed export, compare only model/build-supported backends. GLM Flash's FP8-KV/TRT-LLM-DSA and BF16-KV/TileLang-DSA are paired treatments: their difference cannot be attributed solely to weight or cache precision.

Measurements and gate. Record loaded methods, scales/exclusions, actual kernels, load/repacking peaks, resident memory, prefill/decode cost and held-out quality. Cover low-load decode and large prefill batches. Check Flash Next main and MTP experts separately before speculation. Reject silent fallback/dequantization or wrong scaling even when text appears plausible. Unsupported combinations remain rejected cells, without forcing a quantization flag. GEXP-PR38726, GEXP-SRC-QUANT, GEXP-GLMFLASH.

G06 — Natural MTP/DSpark acceptance and load crossover

Question and treatments. After G01, compare target-only with the existing Qwen 27B/GLM Flash MTP and Kimi DSpark profiles. Sweep only depths supported by the pinned recipe/build, holding MTP top-k at one. DeepSeek 0731 uses bundled DSpark rather than Kimi's external drafter. Fix target sampling, reasoning effort and topology; the atlas's plain Kimi target-only and DCP speculative profiles do not by themselves isolate speculation.

Measurements and gate. Record accepted/proposed draft tokens, verification calls, committed tokens, the bonus-token convention, draft/verify time, queueing and state memory. Separate coding, tools, short answers and long reasoning across feasible concurrency. Exercise rejection, cancellation and resumed prefixes. Reject state or protocol errors and quality regression. High acceptance alone does not establish lower total cost. Current GLM Flash speculative paths exclude DP-attention, so that combination is not a valid cell. GEXP-KIMI, GEXP-DEEPSEEK, GEXP-GLMFLASH.

G07 — Graph replay, input preparation and prefill interference

Question and treatments. Compare normal graph execution with the build's supported eager control, then vary chunked-prefill size at fixed concurrency/context. Resolve flags against that build: current docs expose per-phase graph controls, while older releases may differ. Test main-only Qwen NEXTN fusion after G05/G06. Trace whether DeepSeek 0731 executes the changed compressed-length helper. Kimi pipeline/dynamic-chunk profiling is a separate topology-qualified subcase after G08.

Measurements and gate. Interleave long incoming prompts and short decodes. Record capture time/memory, graph hits/fallbacks, CPU launch/synchronization cost, prefill duration and decode tail latency. Exercise batch transitions, padding, final partial chunks, supported custom positions/images and aborts. Reject replay/eager quality divergence outside tolerance, mapping overruns, profiling leaks or SLO failure. The Kimi patch's synthetic six-layer B200 check does not qualify full K3. GEXP-ARGS, GEXP-PR41175, GEXP-PR43604, GEXP-PR44557.

G08 — Eight-GPU allocation and expert-routing correctness

Question and treatments. For qualified Qwen/Gemma paths compare eight TP1 replicas with four TP2 or two TP4 groups when supported. Compare GLM Flash two-TP4 versus TP8. Reduce Mistral TP only after real-weight capacity and correctness gates. Retain Kimi's eight-GPU target until a smaller complete group qualifies. Vary MoE EP/DP/TP only within documented combinations, recording attention/expert groups and actual GPU membership. EP can reuse another group and is not necessarily another multiplicative GPU dimension.

Measurements and gate. Compare total-node goodput, per-rank memory/expert load, collective time/bytes, routing imbalance and cache locality at equal offered load. Include unequal batches and skewed traffic. Regress main #40498's padding sentinel when local expert mapping is active; mark it inapplicable for a backend bypassing that mapping. Reject missing/double reductions, bad padding, rank hangs and layouts exceeding eight GPUs. GEXP-SRC-EP, GEXP-PR40498.

G09 — Four-plus-four prefill/decode separation

Upstream baseline. The cookbook's preview mechanically exercises TP2 prefill + TP2 decode on four GB300 GPUs with dummy weights. It uses NIXL transfer, BF16 KV, TileLang for both DSA phases and the Triton MoE runner. It provides no real-weight load or accuracy result. The Playground's Mooncake selection is a separate path, and the ordinary Blackwell flashinfer_trtllm MoE runner is explicitly untested under PD. GEXP-GLMFLASH.

Question and treatments. The TP4 prefill + TP4 decode layout is this atlas's proposed eight-GPU adaptation. Qualify it with real weights using the preview's NIXL/BF16-KV/TileLang-DSA/Triton-MoE combination. Keep speculation and prefill CP disabled and the two roles at equal TP size. Give the roles distinct NCCL ports, match the router's prefill bootstrap port, and retain the documented single-node NIXL settings in both environments: UCX_NET_DEVICES=lo and UCX_TLS=tcp,cuda_copy,cuda_ipc,self,sm. Compare against two colocated TP4 replicas with the same KV/DSA/MoE settings to isolate disaggregation. Test alternate backends or Mooncake separately after qualification; compare TP8 colocated separately. Kimi with eight GPUs per role exceeds the node budget and cannot be relabeled 4+4 without new capacity/support evidence.

Measurements and gate. Sweep prompt/output mixture and arrival rate. Record transfer bytes/time, bootstrap latency, queues and idle time per role, HBM and client goodput. Cover prefix hits/misses, cancellation during transfer, role failure and subsequent requests. Validate every required state component/layout; use transfer checksums where supported. Reject stale/incomplete state, unbounded queues or worse whole-service goodput despite faster isolated decode. Dummy-weight checks cannot pass this gate. GEXP-GLMFLASH, GEXP-PD.

G10 — Main-only SWA tail backups

Question and treatments. After G03 qualifies an SWA component in buffer_only host mode, compare default off with SGLANG_ENABLE_HICACHE_SWA_TAIL_ONLY_BACKUP=1 on a build containing #44173. Fix pool/storage settings and confirm the intended code path. Gemma's architectural sliding window alone does not prove support for this runtime/pool combination.

Measurements and gate. Cover short/long nodes, splits, internal-prefix hits and distinct window sizes. Retain bytes and operations per pool, prefetch stalls, recomputed tokens and TTFT. Force misses and interrupted writes, then compare continuations with an uncached reference. Reject false storage-hit beliefs, missing sidecars, wrong tails or quality loss. Adopt reduced I/O only when it improves the real workload. GEXP-PR44173, GEXP-SRC-SWA.

G11 — Conditional Confidential Computing scheduler probe

Question and treatments. Inventory CC status first; mark inapplicable when absent. In an existing CC environment, compare a qualified pre-change build with one containing #36810 under the same CC mode/dependencies. Verify worker selection. Do not change the server's security mode as a performance treatment.

Measurements and gate. Trace result D2H copies, scheduler launch/idle intervals, queues and generation latency. Cover all ranks, logprobs if used, cancellation and copy failure. Check device ownership and error propagation instead of consumption of invalid host results. The implementation moves copying off the scheduling thread; it supplies no universal B300 speed claim. GEXP-PR36810, GEXP-SRC-CC.

G12 — Optional open-PR reasoning-history probe

Question and treatments. For an application using Anthropic Messages, compare a qualified baseline against an isolated build containing PR #40965's observed patch 9f9415c5072aaf89f3359c238649eac7bc85acc7. Record base, patch and resulting build identities. Recheck status before execution: it was open at retrieval and is not an ordinary nightly feature.

Measurements and gate. With fixed Qwen template/history inspect rendered next-turn input for reasoning/content separation, thinking-only turns, ordinary text and tool responses. Then evaluate multi-turn quality and token budgets, retaining an equivalent OpenAI route as a reference if applicable. Reject duplicate/lost reasoning, malformed history and increased truncation. This is protocol correctness, with no serving-speed hypothesis. GEXP-PR40965, GEXP-PR40965-PATCH.

Interpreting results

Run G01 before optimization. Select follow-ups from measured constraints: G02/G04 for state pressure, G03/G10 for reuse/I/O, G05 for exports/kernels, G06/G07 for decode/scheduling, and G08/G09 for node allocation. G11/G12 require their stated environments. Test interactions only after individual treatments pass and record each combination separately. Update a profile's evidence only with actual qualified results; retain unsuccessful runs and exact provenance.

frameworks/vllm/EXPERIMENTS.md

vLLM experiments for one 8×B300 node

Status: designed, not executed. These experiments use the fresh source review, the atlas's immutable checkpoint records and its existing launch candidates. They propose measurements on the actual node; no throughput, accuracy, memory-fit or image-compatibility result is implied.

Use the cross-engine roadmap to choose the next experiment and the experiment manifest template to record its control, candidate, gates and results.

Common controls and decision rule

Record the engine source commit and artifact checksum/image digest; CUDA, driver, PyTorch, FlashInfer, DeepGEMM and NCCL versions; model/export/drafter revisions; actual GPU topology; resolved arguments; kernel selection; and every workload/cache setting. Use the same tokenizer, chat template, reasoning budget, sampling, parser, payload hashes and CPU allocation across an A/B pair. The observed v0.31.0 tag, current-main commit and nightly index point to different artifacts; see the version ledger.

Declare the application's quality threshold and p95/p99 latency budgets before measuring. Primary outcome is whole-node goodput meeting those budgets, with successful output tokens per second, TTFT, generation latency, memory and errors reported alongside it. A lower median with worse tail latency can lose this decision. Keep offered load and failed requests in the denominator. SSE chunk gaps are not exact token latency; use server traces for kernel and token timings. Follow the full benchmark protocol.

Use a staged workload: tokenized inputs of 4,096, 16,384, 32,768 and 61,440 tokens, with 128 or 1,024 requested output tokens, where the checkpoint and application support them. Count templates, images, reasoning and output against the configured context cap; shorten cells that would exceed it. Start at concurrency 1, then 8 and 32, then the measured saturation region. Add a controlled arrival trace for queueing, since the bundled HTTP probe is closed-loop. Interleave at least three independent A/B repetitions and report dispersion and all failures. Increase repetitions when uncertainty is material to the decision.

For every experiment, hard failures include nonfinite outputs, invalid supported protocol responses, incorrect cache/state reuse, OOM, hangs and unrecovered rank failure. Quality is evaluated on representative held-out tasks, including tool/JSON and multilingual cases where needed. Set the allowable quality change before the run. A failed or unsupported cell is a result with a reason; it is never silently removed from the comparison.

Coverage and order

Model familyStart fromHighest-value follow-ups after V01
Kimi K3Native TP8 candidate; evaluate the patched NVIDIA export as a distinct artifactV02 mixed-format dispatch, V03 hybrid state, V05 qualified drafter, V10 startup residency
GLM 5.3FP8 TP8 baseline, then MTP5V02 FP8/NVFP4 quality, V07 metadata, V08 topology
GLM 5.3 FlashFP8 baselineV03 state, V05 MTP, V08 two TP4 groups, V09 4+4 prefill/decode
DeepSeek V4 Flash 0731Four-GPU TP1/DP4+EP, then bundled DSparkV04/V05 draft budgeting and correctness, V08 eight-GPU adaptation
Qwen 3.8 27BBF16 TP1V02 precision, V03 state if the selected backend uses that path, V04 low-concurrency overhead, V08 replicas
Qwen 3.8 Flash NextNVIDIA TP8 baseline, then one-token MTP with EPV03 state, V06 draft head, V08 topology after full checkpoint load accounting
Gemma 4 31B ITBF16 TP1V02 precision, V04 graph/batch behavior, V08 replicas, V10 restart; V06 excludes Gemma 4 MTP
Mistral Medium 3.5Publisher-derived TP8 baseline, then TP2 probeV04 mixed prefill/decode, V08 shard-versus-replica capacity, V10 restart

The table selects experiments from existing model reports. It does not establish that every family reaches every generic vLLM kernel. Runtime class, dtype and backend evidence decide applicability.

V01 — qualify stable and a pinned candidate

Question: Does the newer artifact correctly serve the exact checkpoint, and does it improve a relevant outcome?

Arms: v0.31.0 with a captured image digest versus one captured main/nightly artifact, initially target-only with the same topology and workload. Start with a TP1 model to exercise the process, then qualify each intended checkpoint separately. An unsupported stable checkpoint is a compatibility result; use a separately qualified reference for quality and do not manufacture an A/B speed ratio.

Measure: Load, repacking, memory profiling, JIT/autotune, graph capture, first successful request and steady-state time separately. Record peak memory per GPU, resolved caches/backends, post-warmup goodput and parser/quality results. Repeated startup near the selected memory budget also checks the cuBLAS handle change. Its merged implementation allocates handles before the initial memory snapshot. VEXP-DIFF-61044

Gate: A source identity or successful health check does not pass model correctness. Require a loaded full checkpoint, valid canary requests and all common quality/failure gates. A stable-versus-main difference includes every changed dependency and default. To attribute a gain to a specific PR, add an isolated parent-versus-merge build pair with the same dependencies and configuration, and record any unavoidable dependency change.

V02 — precision and actual kernel dispatch

Question: Does the chosen export/backend trade acceptable quality for more useful node capacity or lower latency?

Arms: Compare the catalogued BF16/FP8/NVFP4 exports available for the family, target-only and with matched effective KV/state precision. Hold topology fixed first. For a given export, separately compare only backends that support its exact weight layout, scaling and activation. Each export is a distinct checkpoint treatment; changing export and backend together cannot isolate either effect.

Measure: Peak load/repacking memory, resident weights and workspaces, cache capacity, kernel names, routing/attention/GEMM time, quality, TTFT and goodput. Include both small batches and long prefills. A smaller file can still require a larger temporary allocation or a slower kernel for the relevant shape.

Gate: Verify the mixed module dtypes rather than labeling the whole checkpoint “4-bit.” Keep Kimi's ModelOpt attention fix and patched-container identity explicit. Treat the new MegaMoE NVFP4 row as a kernel integration research candidate until an exact model caller exists: the inspected change retains MXFP4 callers and accepts only SwiGLU for the NVFP4 row. The source's activation guard rules out inferring Kimi SiTU support from that row. VEXP-52406 VEXP-SRC-MEGA

V03 — hybrid prefix reuse and recurrent-state boundaries

Question: Does shared-prefix caching reduce repeated-prefill work while preserving the correct recurrent state under churn?

Arms: On one qualified artifact, compare cache disabled, a cold cache and a warmed shared prefix. Then vary the supported shared-checkpoint setting while keeping memory and workload fixed. Use workload cohorts with 0%, 50% and 90% shared input prefixes; replay identical cohorts in each arm. Start target-only; add speculation only after state correctness passes. The current stable option is --enable-mamba-shared-prefix-checkpoint; check its effective value on the selected binary. VEXP-REL

Measure: Prefix hit tokens, actual recomputed tokens, state/checkpoint allocations, evictions, TTFT and tail goodput. Inspect a trace to determine whether the fused align-mode gather is used. To isolate that change, compare its parent/merge source under the same cache mode; an absent call is “not applicable.” The upstream result concerns an isolated kernel on MI355X. VEXP-58737

Gate: Use prefix lengths one token before, at and after the resolved block/checkpoint boundary. Force divergence after a shared prefix, eviction, cancellation, request reorder and small/large batch transitions. Compare each continuation with a fresh uncached execution. A high attention-cache hit rate cannot establish recurrent-state correctness. Include non-contiguous block-table views in the kernel qualification; the inspected implementation explicitly handles both strides. VEXP-SRC-STATE

V04 — admission, mixed workloads and CUDA graphs

Question: Which scheduling budget meets the declared tail-latency goal, and does graph replay improve it enough to justify its memory/startup cost?

Arms: Sweep --max-num-batched-tokens through 2,048, 8,192 and 16,384 after memory qualification, holding the sequence cap fixed. Then sweep active-request admission separately, using the supported --max-num-active-seqs control. Compare a homogeneous decode cohort with a fixed mixed trace containing short interactive requests and long prefills. The smaller budget can reduce decode interference while making prefills wait longer. VEXP-OPT VEXP-REL

For the graph comparison, keep compilation/warmup settings fixed and compare resolved NONE with supported captured modes such as FULL_DECODE_ONLY or FULL_AND_PIECEWISE. Record fallback and padded batch sizes. --enforce-eager changes more than graph replay, so label that as a separate debug/startup arm. VEXP-GRAPH

Measure and gate: Track queue depth/time, running requests, preemptions/recomputation, graph memory/capture time, launch overhead, p99 generation latency and goodput. Exercise batch sizes immediately around captured sizes and transitions between prefill and decode. Reject stale replay outputs and memory failures. For DSpark, add a small-budget stress cell only after V05 passes, record the resolved draft-buffer request cap, and avoid interpreting the new admission rule as unlimited concurrency. VEXP-DIFF-59468

V05 — speculation must remain correct and pay for itself

Question: At what load and prompt type does natural speculative acceptance improve service goodput?

Arms: Target-only versus the exact model's qualified DSpark/MTP path on the same artifact and GPUs. DeepSeek 0731 starts from the bundled-DSpark profile; GLM 5.3 starts from MTP5; Qwen Flash Next starts from its one-token MTP profile. Treat other proposal depths, verification policies and greedy/probabilistic drafting as separate supported settings. Preserve the target sampler throughout a pair. No simulated-acceptance environment or config is admissible.

Measure: Accepted draft tokens, proposed tokens, verification steps, effective proposal lengths, drafting/verification time, memory and queueing. Compute acceptance with explicit denominators: mean emitted tokens per verification step includes the bonus token, whereas accepted draft tokens do not. The documented per-request metrics can be reconciled with aggregate counters for an all-n=1 workload; verify their schema on the pinned binary. VEXP-ACCEPT

Gate: Qualify the post-#61161 sampling behavior with repeated verification rounds and the upstream distribution regression, then the model's held-out quality workload. A stochastic output-string change across versions alone is not a quality failure; reuse of conditioned random draws can bias the distribution. Cover seed replay within one configuration, reordered requests and split batches. VEXP-61161

For resumable input sessions, stop inside an accepted draft run using EOS, a stop token and output-length limits, then send another input chunk. Verify that every new chunk token is computed correctly under synchronous/asynchronous scheduling and after cancellation. This is the #58668 regression surface; a normal output-streaming smoke request does not cover it. Retain target-only as the reference for capacity and quality even if speculation wins concurrency 1. VEXP-DIFF-58668

V06 — quantize an eligible draft head without changing the target

Question: Does reducing output-head work in the drafter compensate for its extra memory and any acceptance change on B300?

Arms: On a build containing #59973, begin with Qwen Flash Next's qualified TP8/MTP1 configuration. Compare the unmodified shared head with draft_lm_head_quantization set to fp8, then nvfp4, inside the existing speculative-config object. Keep proposal depth, target/export, target sampler, graphs and kernel settings fixed. Increase proposal depth only in a later sweep. Require Model Runner V2 and a loader-confirmed eligible shared head; configuration acceptance alone is insufficient. VEXP-59973

Mechanism: The target retains its head for verification. The drafter gets a per-rank copy: FP8 uses weight/activation quantization, whereas this NVFP4 path uses W4A16. The selected kernel matters at small row counts. Inspect its name and shape rather than importing DGX Spark/GB10 speed ratios. VEXP-SRC-DRAFTHEAD

Measure and gate: Head time, total draft time, natural acceptance, added resident and peak-load memory, cache capacity and whole-node goodput at concurrency 1/8/32. Use multilingual and tool/JSON prompts to expose proposal changes. Run the V05 quality/state gates. If weight reload is part of the intended service, verify copy refresh with graph replay. Do not apply this option to DSpark, DFlash or Gemma 4 MTP: the inspected config excludes them. VEXP-SRC-SPECCONFIG

V07 — GLM sparse-MLA metadata update

Question: Does the recent in-place metadata change reduce measured draft overhead for the exact GLM deployment?

Arms: Isolate #61873 with equivalent builds immediately before and after its merge, using GLM 5.3 FP8 and MTP5. Start at the atlas TP8 layout. A TP4 follow-up is a separately qualified topology cell. Keep DCP and PCP equal to 1, HiSparse absent, and confirm that FlashInfer sparse MLA plus the metadata-update path are selected. Source guards deliberately exclude other combinations. VEXP-DIFF-61873

Measure: CPU preparation, GPU metadata kernel time, draft-step time, natural acceptance and end-to-end goodput. Include concurrency 1, 8 and 32, mixed prompt lengths and graph batch transitions. The upstream PR reports a small Blackwell result on a narrow workload; interleaved repeated runs are needed to resolve an effect of that size locally. VEXP-61873

Gate: Check token-to-request mapping after reorder, preemption, cancellation, partial prefill and graph replay. Compare quality and acceptance against the control. Do not credit the change when the relevant backend/guard disables it. Extend to GLM Flash only after tracing its exact backend; the shared family name is not proof that the same path is active.

V08 — allocate all eight GPUs fairly

Question: Does the workload benefit more from cooperating ranks or additional independently schedulable replicas?

CandidateWhole-node comparison, after each layout fits and passes correctness
Qwen 27B / Gemma 31BEight TP1 replicas vs four TP2 replicas vs two TP4 replicas
Mistral MediumOne TP8 instance vs two TP4 instances; four TP2 instances only after its capacity probe passes
GLM 5.3 NVFP4 / GLM FlashOne TP8 instance vs two TP4 instances of the same exact export
DeepSeek 0731Two independent copies of the four-GPU TP1/DP4+EP baseline vs one TP1/DP8+EP adaptation; record the single four-GPU baseline separately

Controls: Match total GPUs, offered node load, routing policy, cache warmness and latency budgets. Record weights/attention replication as well as expert sharding. EP uses TP×DP ranks; it does not multiply that GPU count again. Test communication backends only after matching their dependency and topology requirements; multi-node-NVLink settings cannot be inferred from ordinary intra-node NVLink. VEXP-EP

Measure and gate: Whole-node goodput, per-replica queues, expert imbalance, communication time, memory per rank, and the worst tail latency. Preserve empty-expert-batch, skewed-routing and rank-failure cases. Requalify native DeepSeek/Kimi MegaMoE callers after the refactor; its kernel tests do not prove multi-rank numerical correctness. NVFP4 row integration remains gated by V02. VEXP-61538

For Kimi and Qwen Flash Next, begin from their full eight-GPU candidates. Smaller layouts require explicit capacity evidence including auxiliary/draft tensors and host allocations; this plan does not infer that a replica layout fits.

V09 — GLM Flash prefill/decode split on the same node

Question: Does a 4-GPU prefill pool plus 4-GPU decode pool improve tail latency enough to pay for transfer, routing and duplicated residency?

Arms: Two independent colocated TP4 services versus one TP4-prefill/TP4-decode pair, all using the same exact GLM Flash export, context cap, KV dtype and MTP setting. The current vLLM recipe uses NIXL, DS convolution-state layout, HND KV layout and matching MTP5 on both roles. The recipe also sets --no-disable-hybrid-kv-cache-manager on both roles; retain and verify its effective value when materializing the manifest. Preserve that contract for the recipe-derived arm. A target-only PD variation is a separate compatibility probe. VEXP-GLMFLASH-RECIPE

Measure: End-to-end TTFT and generation tail latency, transfer volume/time, state serialization, CPU work, both queues, resident memory and node goodput. Use the same routed arrival trace, sweeping long-prefill share and prefix reuse. Capture both raw completion throughput and SLO-conforming goodput; phase separation is not evidence of a raw-throughput gain. VEXP-PD

Gate: Confirm transfer/reconstruction of attention, sparse-index, recurrent/convolutional and required draft state for this exact path. Compare post-transfer continuations with colocated execution. Interrupt transfer, cancel the requester, restart a role and exercise eviction; require correct fallback/rejection without stale state or a stuck queue. Confirm effective graph modes separately because the recipe gives the roles different startup settings. Keep the result at engine level until a separate KServe lifecycle experiment qualifies the service orchestration.

V10 — restart time and the cost of retained GPU weights

Question: Is retaining GPU weights across restarts worth its residency and lifecycle cost?

Arms: On one qualified artifact, compare ordinary disk loading with vllm preload plus the documented ipc_cache loader. Start with a TP1 target, then repeat for the intended sharded layout if every quantization method supports pre-processed IPC weights. Hold compile/autotune cache state fixed. Time initial daemon preparation separately, and include it when calculating the total cost of a restart sequence. Do not reuse one daemon across stable/main builds with mismatching fingerprints. VEXP-PRELOAD

Measure and gate: Time readiness through the first correct canary, not merely process start. Record retained HBM, peak restart HBM, weight mapping, compilation, graph capture and the lost capacity for other replicas. Exercise a missing daemon, wrong revision/topology, cold compile cache and a rejected unsupported quantizer. Distinguish disk fallback from an IPC cache hit in the logs. In default zero-copy mode, the daemon must survive the engine and sleep-mode weight offload is unsupported. Requalify the complete target/drafter pair when applicable.

Initialized-engine snapshots are an optional separate experiment for the documented one-GPU/TP1 environment. They require host/driver/CRIU compatibility, exclude speculation, and have only dense-FP16 validation in the cited guide. They do not replace the TP8 restart experiment. Qualify a candidate model separately, include creation cost and disk footprint, check the restored canary, and test rejection after a changed runtime identity. No host privilege or kernel changes were performed by this atlas. VEXP-SNAPSHOT

What a completed experiment must retain

Save the control and candidate manifests, artifact identities, payload hashes, exact launch arguments, effective engine configuration, target/draft provenance, node inventory, quality outputs, metrics/traces and raw failures. Include each run's timing boundaries and cache state. Record “not applicable” when a feature's source/runtime guard excludes the cell, “failed” when a gate fails, and “inconclusive” when uncertainty prevents a decision.

Promote a candidate only when its correctness and quality gates pass and the predeclared service objective improves by the required margin across the intended workload. Retain the qualified control artifact and configuration for rollback. These plans supply evidence to choose an engine and deployment; they contain no measured winner.

profiles/README.md

Launch experiments, not performance promises

The profile index lists all 34 experiments.

Every JSON file is populated with a model, variant, engine, GPU topology, arguments, evidence and limitations. None was run on a B300 in this environment. candidate means an evidence-informed experiment; compatibility-probe means an additional unresolved loader or API gate; blocked cannot be executed by the launcher.

The launcher prints a command by default. Execution requires a local model directory containing config.json and a superstorm-revision.json provenance file whose immutable Hugging Face commit matches the profile's model_revision. Produce the metadata with scripts/fetch_metadata.py --ref <model_revision>, then separately obtain authorized weights for that revision. This repository does not download weights. An external drafter needs its own directory and matching draft_revision provenance. The launcher rejects snapshots marked INCOMPLETE.txt.

python scripts/launch.py --profile profiles/qwen3.8-27b.bf16.vllm.baseline.json
python scripts/launch.py --help

For an actual launch, add --execute --accept-unbenchmarked --model-dir /models/exact-revision. Probe profiles additionally need --allow-compatibility-probe. Any remote-code enablement needs --allow-reviewed-remote-code; audit the code at that revision first. Do not expose the service publicly without an authenticated gateway.

auto cache precision can resolve differently across exports. The Qwen 27B SGLang profiles explicitly choose BF16 KV as a local controlled comparison; the current cookbook pins FP8 E4M3 KV. These profiles do not reproduce its measurements. Record the vLLM effective dtype and change it deliberately for a matched comparison. Weight precision is independent of KV and recurrent-state precision. Effective engine help, loader logs and correctness tests remain authoritative; CLI flags were not exercised against installed engines here.

The Qwen Flash Next SGLang probe follows the exact NVIDIA TP1 recipe added in PR 41046, whose source marks the cell verified:false. Its 64K context cap, 32-request cap and 0.82 memory fraction are local unbenchmarked additions. The GLM 5.3 SGLang profile uses glm47 for tool calls and glm45 for reasoning, as required by its cookbook.

Do not run all profiles simultaneously. gpu_count is the GPU allocation for one instance, not a promise all requested models fit together. Independent one-GPU replicas need distinct GPU assignments and ports. Expert parallelism redistributes existing ranks: it is not another GPU-count multiplier.

Source recipes sometimes use more aggressive graph, symmetric-memory and pool settings. Those are intentionally not copied into every baseline. See the model's serving report and benchmark protocol.

Back to contents ↑
reports/DECISIONS.md

Decision record

ADR 001: Separate model architecture from export producer

The same family can have native, NVIDIA, RedHat and Inferact artifacts with different quantization contracts. Keep each under the originating company and record producer-specific repository IDs. Rejected alternative: one flat “FP4 models” list that hides lineage and loader differences.

ADR 002: Use evidence stages instead of a binary supported flag

A model can be registered yet fail to import, load mixed scales, preserve state or parse tool calls. Record source evidence, compatibility probes, blocked paths and local benchmark status separately. Rejected alternative: a model-card widget means full production support.

ADR 003: Do not select eight-way tensor parallelism by habit

Large MoE targets and small dense targets stress different resources. Compare exact TP/DP/EP layouts and per-GPU replicas under the same node-wide workload. Rejected alternative: every model must use all eight GPUs in one tensor-parallel group.

ADR 004: Keep the control plane separate from the inference runtime

KServe/Kubernetes govern placement, health and rollout; vLLM/SGLang execute numerical inference. First validate a one-node target-only runtime, then add orchestration and only measured-benefit disaggregation. Rejected alternative: use a control-plane product as evidence of a faster kernel.

ADR 005: Metadata and image pins precede production

Resolve mutable model IDs and nightly tags to immutable identities, preserve raw metadata and compare outputs. Keep current source/release observations without claiming tested nightly binaries. Rejected alternative: a moving latest command is reproducible.

ADR 006: State what each parameter count includes

Keep publisher headlines, main-model counts, serialized auxiliary parameters and stored tensor bytes separate. GLM 5.3’s MTP tensors explain why its approximately 743B main model has a larger checkpoint count. DeepSeek’s competing recipe headlines still lack a reconciled exact census. Uniform byte estimates using rounded counts remain illustrations; runtime memory additionally depends on the loader, sharding, caches and workspaces.

These are engineering decisions derived from the framework comparison, source review and memory analysis. They are not empirical performance results.

Back to contents ↑
reports/EXPERIMENTS.md

Experiment roadmap: stable engines, main and nightly

This is a planned experiment program, with no GPU results yet. It turns the current vLLM, SGLang and KServe research into an order of work on the eight-GPU B300 baseline. Feature details and primary citations live in those engine pages; the nightly report identifies the observed source and artifact versions.

What each comparison can tell us

ComparisonHold fixedWhat a result would support
Stable A versus pinned nightly B, same engineExact checkpoint, workload, effective precision, topology and service objectiveWhether the complete candidate build improves this workload. Different bundled dependencies prevent attributing the change to one PR.
Candidate build with feature off versus onSame build and all other effective settingsWhether that feature helps within its supported combinations. Record any settings it automatically changes.
vLLM versus SGLangCheckpoint/export, tokenized request content, hardware budget, quality and latency requirementsWhich complete serving configuration meets this application's needs. Engines need not use identically named kernels or identical scheduling defaults.
Direct engine versus KServe-managed endpointEngine image, model, replicas, request payloads and resource allocationAdded routing/lifecycle cost or benefit. Keep gateway behavior separate from numerical-engine behavior.

Start with one fixed-configuration comparison, then tune each engine within the same hardware and objective. Report both results. This separates an upgrade regression from the best configuration found under an explicitly equal tuning budget. A winner found for 4K coding prompts is not a winner for 64K document work.

Sequence the work

IDExperiment and hypothesisChange deliberatelyObserve and decide
X01Artifact and load qualification. A stable or nightly build can load the exact export and select the intended execution path.Stable and one pinned candidate build, target-only, short context, low loadRecord import/load/warmup failures, actual kernels, effective dtypes and peak memory. Stop on fallback surprises or incorrect output before comparing speed.
X02Precision and quality. Lower weight or cache precision can create useful capacity while meeting the quality threshold.Weight export first; KV dtype in a separate comparison; state precision only where explicitly supportedHold evaluation data and sampling fixed. Record quality deltas, failure categories and memory at load and steady state. A different export is a different artifact with its own revision.
X03Cache and hybrid-state correctness. Prefix reuse saves prefill without stale state or excess pool pressure.Cold, repeated-prefix and evicted-prefix requests; cache feature off/on where supportedCompare first-answer/TTFT, reuse counters, recurrent/KV allocation, eviction, cancellation and subsequent answers. Interleave unrelated sessions and differing images/tools.
X04Scheduling and graphs. Admission and prefill policies trade queueing against decode interference.One supported scheduling knob at a time; graph policy separatelyReplay a mixed short/long trace. Measure offered/completed load, queue delay, TTFT, TPOT, errors and SLO goodput. Check that the load generator is not the bottleneck.
X05Node-wide parallelism. Communication savings can outweigh larger per-worker batches or replica count.Supported TP/DP/EP arrangements within the same eight-GPU budgetReport whole-node goodput, per-request tails, expert imbalance/communication time and memory. Count all replicas and router resources. Do not compare eight replicas against a single worker as equal-resource results.
X06Natural speculation. Accepted draft work can reduce latency after paying draft, verification and state-management costs.Target-only versus the supported MTP/DSpark path; then supported draft depthRecord acceptance definition and distribution, target/draft work, memory and quality at both low and high load. Reject synthetic acceptance and fake KV runs as service evidence.
X07Startup and recovery. A supported warm-weight or cache mechanism may shorten recovery but retain resources.Ordinary restart versus the documented mechanism, same checkpoint and buildSeparate image fetch, storage read, deserialization/repacking, compilation, graph capture and readiness. Measure time to first correct response and retained GPU/host memory. Test model-version replacement.
X08Prefill/decode separation. Isolation may improve tail latency enough to pay transfer and routing costs.Colocated service versus a supported split with the same total GPU budgetInclude KV and required recurrent-state transfer, fabric traffic, router overhead, failures and placement. Proceed only when the exact architecture/connector combination is documented and qualifies.
X09Service lifecycle. KServe routing and scaling can sustain the qualified engine under variable load.Add one control-plane capability at a time after a direct-engine baselineMeasure gateway overhead, cold starts, burst recovery, cache locality, drain/cancellation and rollback. Treat multi-node expansion as a separate hardware experiment.

X01 is a prerequisite for every other row. X02 and X03 gate performance claims; X06 follows a target-only baseline. X08 is optional, after X04/X05 identify the bottleneck. X09 uses a qualified engine image. These are proposed engineering gates, not upstream guarantees.

Which model to use first

Atlas familyUseful first questionExisting starting point and limit
Qwen 3.8 27BCan a small hybrid target expose state/cache/scheduling regressions cheaply, then scale by replication?BF16 vLLM profile, BF16 SGLang profile; compare the exact FP8/NVFP4 exports only after baseline quality.
Gemma 4 31B ITHow do export precision, multimodal processing and replicas change latency and quality?BF16 vLLM, SGLang NVFP4 probe; a speculative feature mentioning another Gemma variant does not qualify this checkpoint or supply a matching drafter.
GLM 5.3 FlashDo sparse/hybrid state and speculative changes preserve long-context correctness?SGLang target-only, MTP candidate, vLLM FP8.
GLM 5.3Can precision or communication changes improve useful capacity for the large target?vLLM FP8, NVFP4, SGLang FP8; align effective KV dtype before attributing a difference to weights.
Qwen 3.8 Flash NextAre PLE/ngram memory, mixed MTP tensors and sparse attention handled correctly?vLLM TP8, SGLang documented TP1 probe; first qualify each, then compare equal node budgets.
Kimi K3Which exact target/drafter and mixed-precision path qualifies before speculation?Native vLLM, NVFP4 SGLang, SGLang DSpark; the generic vLLM NVFP4 path remains subject to its existing block.
DeepSeek V4 Flash 0731How do DP/EP and DSpark behave for the July-31 architecture?vLLM EP4, local SGLang TP8 target-only probe; the publisher TP4 + DSpark route is a distinct experiment described in the serving report. V4.1-specific improvements cannot be assumed to apply.
Mistral Medium 3.5Is lower TP or more replication useful after the publisher topology qualifies?vLLM TP8, TP2 probe, SGLang.

Profile names are lookup aids; their status, notes and launch gates still apply. This roadmap creates no additional supported configuration and does not remove any blocked/probe status.

For a first campaign, qualify Qwen 3.8 27B BF16 on one GPU with each engine's stable build, then its pinned candidate. Use that manageable target for the shared client, cache, cancellation and scheduler checks. Next use GLM Flash for the model-specific hybrid/MTP questions, and a qualified large MoE for communication experiments. Bring Kimi NVFP4 forward when its exact mixed-format compatibility is the deployment decision. Add KServe using an already qualified small-model service before introducing distributed topology. This order is a proposed way to reduce debugging cost, not a claim that one model predicts another's performance.

Workloads and measurement design

Use three workload classes: interactive short input/output; long input with short answers; and long generation such as coding or reasoning. Add the real application trace, tool/JSON requests and image workloads only for supported models. Keep a separate held-out quality set. Generated random tokens are useful for shape/capacity experiments but do not establish application quality or realistic speculative acceptance.

Choose from the protocol's 4K/16K/32K/64K input buckets only where the complete serialized input, including chat-template and image tokens, plus the requested output fits the actual profile and model context limits. A 32K cap does not admit a 32K input plus generation. Reduce or skip infeasible points; raising a context cap is a separate capacity and accuracy experiment. Screen at concurrency 1 and 8 before larger queues. For saturation, choose a fixed offered request-rate ladder from a pilot and reuse it across A/B runs; include a mixed workload and a burst/recovery trace. Record client queueing when a concurrency cap prevents the intended arrivals from reaching the service.

Warm up explicitly, then randomize A/B order and repeat independently. Three runs can screen candidates; use more repetitions and enough requests to characterize tails before promotion. Publish sample counts and run-to-run variation; a handful of requests cannot establish reliable p99 latency. Retain every failed run and distinguish setup failures from timed service failures. Do not average p99 values and present the result as a pooled p99.

The current vLLM benchmark CLI exposes arrival-rate controls, percentile selection and latency-budget goodput options. SGLang's serving harness supports both its native endpoint and OpenAI-compatible backends. Their defaults and token accounting differ, so use the same frozen client/payload path for the principal cross-engine comparison and native tools for diagnosis. Inspect the installed build's help before choosing arguments; these are mutable documentation observations. XEXP-VBENCH, XEXP-GBENCH.

Only after a pinned environment is prepared, inspect its available interfaces:

vllm bench serve --help
python -m sglang.bench_serving --help

The atlas streaming client is useful for completion/error behavior and a shared client view. It is closed-loop and does not supply an open-loop saturation trace, exact token emission timing or a quality evaluator. Native counters should be reconciled with endpoint usage and tokenizer identity rather than silently mixed.

SGLang documents --fake-prefill as locally generated fake KV for a decode-only microbenchmark. Keep such results explicitly separate from end-to-end, quality, transfer and speculation claims. Similarly, an engine-reported acceptance average needs its denominator and observation interval; a cumulative server counter can contain earlier runs. XEXP-GBENCH.

Record each run before executing it

Copy experiment.template.json into the operator's run directory. It is a documentation template, not an executable launch profile and not consumed by the launcher or benchmark client. Null values are unresolved inputs or unmeasured results, never zero. Fill every applicable identity, control and promotion threshold before a run; mark inapplicable fields with a written reason. Keep credentials out of the record.

Retain the full resolved command and only relevant, nonsecret environment settings; effective backend/graph/dtype diagnostics; model/drafter/tokenizer revisions; image manifest and architecture-specific digest or verified wheel hash; dependency/build versions; node inventory and topology; workload/payload hashes; raw measurements; quality outputs; server logs; and a decision with failure reasons. A release tag, main SHA and nightly tag are three distinct identities.

Predeclare the minimum quality score, permitted quality regression, latency budgets, error budget, minimum useful goodput improvement, capacity margin and analysis method. Specify how repetitions are paired or aggregated and how uncertainty is estimated. The template supplies no invented business SLA. Promote only a configuration that meets those requirements across repeated runs, including cancellation, eviction and recovery. Compare uncertainty in the baseline-to-candidate improvement with the predeclared useful-improvement threshold: an interval that straddles that threshold is inconclusive; an interval entirely below it fails the improvement requirement. Overlap between raw run ranges alone does not decide this comparison. Retain the baseline artifact for rollback.

Deliverables

For each completed experiment produce a filled manifest, raw per-request records, quality report and a short decision table: baseline versus candidate, hardware consumed, quality, goodput under the declared SLO, latency tails, peak memory, errors, and startup/recovery where relevant. Explain why a candidate wins or fails for that workload. The engine pages describe the specific current changes worth testing; none of the planned rows is a measured result.

scripts/README.md

Utilities and trust boundaries

All tools use the Python standard library. launch.py requires a separately installed engine only when execution is explicitly requested. No script installs dependencies or model weights automatically. Use Python 3.10 or newer; local tests were run on the version reported in VALIDATION.md.

ToolDefault behaviorExplicit side effect
memory.pyCalculate illustrative payload bytesNone
preflight.pyRead available node/tool metadata--output writes a new inventory file
launch.pyPrint guarded command--execute starts a local, offline engine after acknowledgements
clone_upstreams.pyPrint exact revision retrieval commands--execute creates new shallow checkouts; no existing repository modified
fetch_metadata.pyList catalog checkpoint IDs--fetch resolves and archives metadata only
validate_repo.pyRead and check local repository integrityNone
build_atlas.pyRebuild and write ATLAS.html from current Markdown sourcesDefault overwrites that generated file; --check is read-only and reports stale output
../benchmarks/run_streaming.pyRequires an explicit endpoint/model/workloadSends those requests and writes a new measurement file
../deploy/kserve/render.pyRequires profile, digest, PVC and resourcesPrints JSON only; never applies

Treat profile JSON and model code as trusted executable inputs: these tools do not sandbox arbitrary profiles or remote model implementations. CLI guardrails prevent common accidental actions, not malicious operator input. The launcher validates checkpoint identity and config metadata hash, not the weight shards. Obtain and independently verify all weights from the same immutable commit. A metadata-only directory cannot successfully serve until the matching authorized weights and any required processor/tokenizer files have been added.

Metadata retrieval intentionally excludes full tokenizer vocabularies, weight files and executable Python implementations. Optional missing files are recorded. API auth uses HF_TOKEN from the environment and strips it across cross-host redirects. The benchmark uses BENCH_API_KEY, rejects credential-bearing URLs and never follows redirects. Neither tool prints tokens or dumps the environment. The metadata downloader is covered by offline mocks; the source HTTP retrievals recorded by this audit are separate from an end-to-end integration test of this downloader against the Hub.

The streaming probe implements the usual one-JSON-object-per-SSE-data-line interface exposed by these chat endpoints and accepts one completion per request (n=1). General multi-line SSE event assembly is outside its scope. It is a closed-loop integration probe, not an open-loop saturation benchmark or exact per-token profiler. Requests may generate server-side costs; the tool only sends the explicit JSONL workload, after validating the workload and opening a new output file. An interrupted process can leave an incomplete output file. Returned tools/code are not executed. --timeout bounds socket operations and is checked between bounded line reads; it is not a strict wall-clock cancellation guarantee.

The launcher rejects synthetic acceptance in both environment settings and speculative configuration. Profile topology/context fields must match their engine arguments. validate_repo.py also checks model/catalog and profile/catalog equality, immutable revision consistency, duplicate identities, uncatalogued model/profile records and strict JSON syntax. These checks do not certify model weights, engine behavior or publisher claims.
